On massive neutral lepton scattering on nucleus
V.A. Bednyakov

Abstract
The paper presents a theoretical approach to the description of the relativistic scattering of a massive (neutral) lepton on a nucleus, in which the latter retains its integrity. The measurable cross section of this process includes the elastic (or coherent) contribution, when the nucleus remains in its original quantum state and the inelastic (incoherent) contribution, when the nucleus goes into another (excited) quantum state. Transition from the elastic scattering regime to the inelastic scattering regime is regulated automatically by the dependence of the nucleon-nucleus form factors on the momentum transferred to the nucleus. At small momentum transfers elastic scattering dominates. AS the transferred momentum increases, the contribution of the inelastic scattering increases, and the latter becomes dominant at sufficiently large transferred momenta. The interaction of a pointlike lepton with structureless nucleons of the target nucleus is parameterized with four effective coupling constants, reflecting the (axial)vector nature of the weak interaction. The scattering of massive (anti)neutrinos interacting with nucleons through the V ∓ A V\mp A currents of the Standard Model is considered in detail. Because of the nonzero masses, an additional channel arises for elastic and inelastic scattering of these (anti)neutrinos on nuclei due to the possibility of changing the helicity of these (anti)neutrinos. For example, despite the smallness of the masses at (kinetic) energies of (anti)neutrinos much lower than the neutrino masses (for example, relic ones), the cross section of their interaction with the nucleus turns out to be many times enhanced, at least due to the ”nucleus coherence effect”. The expressions obtained for the cross sections are applicable to any precision data analysis involving neutrinos and antineutrinos, especially when non-zero neutrino masses can be taken into account. These expressions can also be used in the analysis of experiments on direct detection of (neutral) massive weakly interacting relativistic dark matter particles since, unlike the generally accepted case, they simultaneously take into account both elastic and inelastic interactions of the particles. The presence of an ”inelastic signal” with its characteristic signature may be the only registrable evidence of interaction of the dark matter particle with the nucleus.



1
Introduction

In
Bednyakov:2018mjd
;
Bednyakov:2019dbl
;
Bednyakov:2021ppn
,
an approach was formulated to describe the interaction of
massless
neutrinos and antineutrinos with a nucleus resulting in that the nucleus either remains in its original state or goes into an excited state while maintaining the integrity
\nu(\bar{\nu})+A\to\nu(\bar{\nu})+A^{(*)}
.
In
Bednyakov:2022dmc
, this approach was generalized to the case of nonrelativistic scattering of a massive neutral weakly interacting lepton on nuclei.
On this basis, the balance of coherence and incoherence was studied in the problem of direct dark matter particle search with allowance for specific kinematic conditions and excitation energy levels of the nucleus.
It was noted that one usually underestimates the role of inelastic processes in this problem
Bednyakov:2023dmc
.

Recall that the approach
Bednyakov:2018mjd
;
Bednyakov:2019dbl
;
Bednyakov:2021ppn

is based on the description of the nucleus as a bound state of its structureless nucleons, whose interaction with the lepton is given by the effective 4-fermionic Lagrangian and is described by means of the scalar products of the lepton and nucleon currents, which allows one to control the spin states of nucleons in the initial and
final states of the nucleus.
Due to the constructive use of the completeness of the wave functions of nuclear states
(conservation of probability), it is possible to represent the observable cross section as a sum of two terms.
One term corresponds to the elastic interaction, in which the initial quantum state of the nucleus is preserved.
The other term is the total contribution to the observable cross section of all other (potentially possible) inelastic processes, which involve a change in the quantum state of the nucleus.

It was found that as the 3-momentum
\bm{q}
transferred to the nucleus increases,
the nuclear form factors of the proton/neutron
F_{p/n}(\bm{q})

automatically regulate
the transition from
the elastic regime to the inelastic regime of the lepton-nucleus interaction.
This phenomenon is the
fundamental difference
of the approach

Bednyakov:2018mjd
;
Bednyakov:2019dbl
;
Bednyakov:2021ppn
from Friedman’s concept of coherence

Freedman:1973yd
;
Freedman:1977xn
, when, based on the result of comparing of the radius of the target nucleus and the characteristic momentum transferred to it, one decided which formula — coherent or incoherent — should be used to calculate the cross section for (anti)neutrino scattering by the nucleus.

It was also noted that elastic and inelastic neutrino
\nu(\bar{\nu})A
processes turn out to be experimentally indistinguishable
when the recoil energy of the nucleus is the only observable quantity.
Therefore, in experiments aimed at studying the coherent scattering of (anti)neutrinos
(at sufficiently high energies) by detecting only the recoil energy of the nucleus
Akimov:2017ade
, an incoherent background can occur that is indistinguishable from the signal
when
\gamma
-quanta de-exciting the nucleus cannot be registered.

A similar situation takes place in the direct detection of dark matter particles.
The incoherent (inelastic) contribution to the expected event rate dominates in a number of kinematic regions
at quite admissible values of the New Physics parameters

Bednyakov:2022dmc
;
Bednyakov:2023dmc
.
This incoherent contribution is of independent interest because it is an additional source
of important information about the nature (unknown in the case of dark matter) of the interaction.
It can be measured directly by detection of photons emitted by the target nuclei excited
as a result of inelastic processes

Bednyakov:2021bty
.
The number of such photons is proportional to the ratio of the cross section of the inelastic interaction channel
to the cross section of the elastic channel, and these photons should have an energy spectrum characteristic of the nucleus
A
, which, as a rule, is much larger than the recoil energy of the nucleus, which could greatly simplifiy their detection.

In this context, the goal of this paper is to further generalize both the
massless neutrino

Bednyakov:2018mjd
;
Bednyakov:2019dbl
;
Bednyakov:2021ppn

and the
massive lepton

Bednyakov:2022dmc
;
Bednyakov:2023dmc
approaches
to the
relativistic
case of interaction of
massive
neutral weakly interacting particles
(
\chi
leptons) with nuclei
\chi A\to\chi A^{(*)}
,
and elucidate new regularities that arise within the generaliztion.

2
Kinematics and cross section of
{\chi A}
scattering

When two particles interact with the formation of two particles,
\chi+A\rightarrow\chi+A^{(*)}
,
the 4-momenta of the incoming and outgoing
\chi
particles are denoted as
k=(k_{0}=E_{\chi},\bm{k})
and
k^{\prime}=(k^{\prime}_{0}=E_{\chi}^{\prime},\bm{k}^{\prime})
, and the 4-momenta of the initial and the final state of the nucleus, respectively, as
P_{n}=(P^{0}_{n},\bm{P}_{n})
and
P^{\prime}_{m}=(P^{0}_{m},\bm{P}_{m})
(Fig.
1
, left).
The total energy of the nuclear state
|P_{n}\rangle
is
P_{n}^{0}=E_{\bm{P}}+\varepsilon_{n}
,
where
\varepsilon_{n}
is the internal energy of the
n
th quantum state of the nucleus.

Figure 1:
An example of
\chi A
-interaction due to the exchange of the neutral
Z
-boson (left).
Kinematics of this process in the laboratory frame where the nucleus
A
is at rest (right).

If the
\chi
particle with mass
m_{\chi}
and momentum
\bm{k}
hits the nucleus
A
at rest along the
z
axis
and flies away at angle
\theta
to the
x
-axis with momentum
\bm{k}^{\prime}
,
then all 4-momenta can be written as

\displaystyle k

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big(k_{0}=\sqrt{m^{2}_{\chi}+|\bm{k}|^{2}},0,0,k_{z}=|\bm{k}|\big),\qquad P_{n}=\big(P_{n}^{0}=m_{A}+\varepsilon_{n},0,0,0\big),

\displaystyle k^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big(k^{\prime}_{0}=\sqrt{m^{2}_{\chi}+|\bm{k}^{\prime}|^{2}},k^{\prime}_{x}=|\bm{k}^{\prime}|\sin\theta,0,k^{\prime}_{z}=|\bm{k}^{\prime}|\cos\theta\big),

\displaystyle P^{\prime}_{m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big(P_{m}^{0}=\varepsilon_{m}+\sqrt{m_{A}^{2}+\bm{q}^{2}},-|\bm{k}^{\prime}|\sin\theta,0,|\bm{k}|-|\bm{k}^{\prime}|\cos\theta\big),

where
m_{A}
is the mass of the
A
nucleus, and
\varepsilon_{m}
is the excitation energy of the
m
th level of this nucleus

Bednyakov:2022dmc
.
The 4-momentum
q=(q_{0},\bm{q})
transferred to the nucleus is related to these quantities in the following way:

\displaystyle q^{2}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle(k-k^{\prime})^{2}=2\big(m^{2}_{\chi}-\sqrt{(m^{2}_{\chi}+|\bm{k}^{\prime}|^{2})(m^{2}_{\chi}+|\bm{k}|^{2})}+|\bm{k}||\bm{k}^{\prime}|\cos\theta\big),

\displaystyle q_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle k_{0}-k^{\prime}_{0}=P^{0}_{m}-P^{0}_{n}=\Delta\varepsilon_{mn}+T_{A},

(1)

\displaystyle\bm{q}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\bm{k}-\bm{k}^{\prime})^{2}=(-|\bm{k}^{\prime}|\sin\theta)^{2}+(|\bm{k}|-|\bm{k}^{\prime}|\cos\theta)^{2}=|\bm{k}|^{2}+|\bm{k}^{\prime}|^{2}-2|\bm{k}||\bm{k}^{\prime}|\cos\theta.

The notation for the energy difference between the nuclear
|m\rangle
and
|n\rangle
states is introduced:

\Delta\varepsilon_{mn}\equiv\varepsilon_{m}-\varepsilon_{n}.

(2)

The kinetic energy of the motion of the recoil nucleus is defined as

T_{A}(|\bm{k}^{\prime}|,\cos\theta)=\sqrt{m^{2}_{A}+|\bm{k}^{\prime}|^{2}+|\bm{k}|^{2}-2|\bm{k}||\bm{k}^{\prime}|\cos\theta}-m_{A}.

(3)

The maximum value of the recoil energy,
T_{A}^{\max}
, corresponds to the maximum momentum transferred, which is obtained at
\cos\theta=-1
, i.e.
\bm{q}^{2}_{\max}=(|\bm{k}|+|\bm{k}^{\prime}|)^{2}
.
As a rule,
T_{A}^{\max}\leq 200
keV, and for the most interesting target nuclei
\Delta\varepsilon_{mn}
is noticeably less than 1 MeV.

Since in the lab frame the target nucleus is at rest in some

|n\rangle
-state, the differential cross section of the process
\chi A_{n}\to\chi A^{(*)}_{m}

(see, for example,

Tanabashi:2018oca
;
Peskin:1995ev
;
Bilenky:1995zq
;
Bednyakov:2018mjd
;
Bednyakov:2019dbl
;
Bednyakov:2021ppn
;
Bednyakov:2022dmc
)
takes the form

\frac{d^{2}\sigma_{mn}}{d|\bm{k}^{\prime}|d\cos\theta}=\frac{-|{i\cal M}_{mn}|^{2}|\bm{k}^{\prime}|^{2}}{2^{5}\pi\sqrt{m^{2}_{\chi}+|\bm{k}^{\prime}|^{2}}}\frac{\delta\Big(k_{0}-\sqrt{m^{2}_{\chi}+|\bm{k}^{\prime}|^{2}}-\Delta\varepsilon_{mn}-T_{A}(|\bm{k}^{\prime}|,\cos\theta)\Big)}{(m_{A}+\varepsilon_{m}+T_{A}(|\bm{k}^{\prime}|,\cos\theta))\sqrt{k^{2}_{0}(m_{A}+\varepsilon_{n})^{2}-m_{\chi}^{2}m_{A}^{2}}}.

(4)

This expression and the recoil energy of the nucleus (
3
) both depend on
momentum
|\bm{k}^{\prime}|
and
\cos\theta
.
Integration over
\cos\theta
is performed using the
\delta
-function of the energy conservation law
from (
4
) represented as a function of
\cos\theta

\delta(f(\cos\theta))=\sum_{i}\frac{\delta(\cos\theta-\cos\theta_{i})}{|df(\cos\theta_{i})/d\ cos\theta|}.

(5)

Here
\cos\theta_{i}
are the solutions (may be not only one) of the equation

f(\cos\theta_{i})\equiv k_{0}-\sqrt{m^{2}_{\chi}+|\bm{k}^{\prime}|^{2}}-\Delta\varepsilon_{mn}-T_{A}(|\bm{k}^{\prime}|,\cos\theta_{i})=0.

(6)

The derivative of this function included in (
5
) is

\dfrac{df(\cos\theta)}{d\cos\theta}=\dfrac{|\bm{k}^{\prime}||\bm{k}|}{T_{A}(|\bm{k}^{\prime}|,\cos\theta)+m_{A}}.

After integrating expression (
4
) over
\cos\theta
one has

\displaystyle\frac{d\sigma_{mn}}{d|\bm{k}^{\prime}|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{-1}{2^{5}\pi\sqrt{k^{2}_{0}(m_{A}+\varepsilon_{n})^{2}-m_{\chi}^{2}m_{A}^{2}}}\frac{|{i\cal M}_{mn}|^{2}|\bm{k}^{\prime}|}{|\bm{k}|\sqrt{m^{2}_{\chi}+|\bm{k}^{\prime}|^{2}}}\frac{m_{A}+T_{A}(|\bm{k}^{\prime}|,\cos\theta_{i})}{(m_{A}+T_{A}(|\bm{k}^{\prime}|,\cos\theta_{i})+\varepsilon_{m})}.\qquad

(7)

Using the Jacobian of the transition from the variable
|\bm{k}^{\prime}|
to the observable variable
T_{A}

\displaystyle\dfrac{dT_{A}(|\bm{k}^{\prime}|,\cos\theta)}{d|\bm{k}^{\prime}|}=-\dfrac{|\bm{k}^{\prime}|}{\sqrt{m^{2}_{\chi}+|\bm{k}^{\prime}|^{2}}},

(8)

from formula (
7
) one gets the
expression for the differential cross section of
\chi A
scattering

\displaystyle\dfrac{d\sigma_{mn}}{dT_{A}}=\dfrac{d\sigma_{mn}}{d|\bm{k}^{\prime}|}\frac{d|\bm{k}^{\prime}|}{dT_{A}}=\frac{|{i\cal M}_{mn}|^{2}}{2^{5}\pi\sqrt{w}}\frac{1}{|\bm{k}|}\frac{T_{A}+m_{A}}{T_{A}+m_{A}+\varepsilon_{m}},

(9)

where the notation for the initial particle flux is

\sqrt{w}\equiv\sqrt{(m^{2}_{\chi}+|\bm{k}|^{2})(m_{A}+\varepsilon_{n})^{2}-m_{\chi}^{2}m_{A}^{2}}\simeq|\bm{k}|m_{A}\sqrt{1+\dfrac{2\varepsilon_{n}}{m_{A}}\Big(1+\dfrac{m^{2}_{\chi}}{|\bm{k}|^{2}}\Big)}.

(10)

Finally, the relativistic cross section of the process
\chi A_{n}\to\chi A_{m}
has the form

\displaystyle\dfrac{d\sigma_{mn}}{dT_{A}}\big(\chi A_{n}\to\chi A_{m}\big)=\frac{|{i\cal M}_{mn}|^{2}}{2^{5}\pi|\bm{k}|^{2}m_{A}}C_{mn}(T_{A}),\qquad\text{where}

(11)

C_{mn}(T_{A})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+\dfrac{2\varepsilon_{n}}{m_{A}}\Big(1+\dfrac{m^{2}_{\chi}}{|\bm{k}|^{2}}\Big)}}\frac{T_{A}+m_{A}}{T_{A}+m_{A}+\varepsilon_{m}}\simeq O(1),

since
m_{A}\gg T_{A}+\varepsilon_{m}
in the considered approximation.

3
The amplitude of
{\chi}
particle scattering on nucleus

The probability amplitude of relativistic
\chi
lepton scattering on a nucleus, as in the nonrelativistic case

Bednyakov:2022dmc
, is based on the assumption that the interaction is between the lepton and the structureless nucleon.
This allows one to use the effective 4-fermion Lagrangian
Bednyakov:2018mjd
;
Bednyakov:2021ppn

written as the product of the lepton
L_{\mu}(x)=h_{\chi}\overline{\psi}_{\chi}(x)O_{\mu}\psi_{\chi}(x)

and nucleon
H^{\mu}(x)=\sum_{f=n,p}h_{f}\overline{\psi}_{f}(x)O^{\mu}\psi_{f}(x)

currents, where the
O^{\mu}
are combinations of
\gamma
-matrices.

The element of the
\mathbb{S}
-matrix,
\langle P^{\prime}_{m},k^{\prime}|\mathbb{S}|P_{n},k\rangle
,
which determines the probability for the nucleus and the
\chi
particle to transform from
the initial state
|P_{n},k\rangle
to the final state
\langle P^{\prime}_{m},k^{\prime}|
due to their interaction
is written in the standard way

\langle P^{\prime}_{m},k^{\prime}|\mathbb{S}|P_{n},k\rangle=(2\pi)^{4}\delta^{4}(q+P_{n}-P^{\prime}_{m})i\mathcal{M}_{mn}=\dfrac{iG_{\rm F}}{\sqrt{2}}\int d^{4}x\,H^{\mu}_{mn}(x)\,L_{\mu}^{\chi}(x),

(12)

where
H^{\mu}_{mn}(x)\equiv\langle P^{\prime}_{m}|H^{\mu}(x)|P_{n}\rangle
is the matrix element of the transition of the nucleus from the state
|P_{n}\rangle
to the state
\langle P^{\prime}_{m}|
due to the hadronic current
H^{\mu}(x)
.
Here, the nuclear state wave function is determined by the expression

Bednyakov:2018mjd
;
Bednyakov:2019dbl
;
Bednyakov:2021ppn
;
Bednyakov:2022dmc

|P_{n}\rangle=\int\Big(\prod^{A}_{i}d\widetilde{\bm{p}}^{\star}_{i}\Big)\frac{\widetilde{\psi}_{n}(\{p^{\star}\})}{\sqrt{{A!}}}\Phi_{n}(p)|\{p^{\star}\}\rangle,\quad\text{where}\quad\bm{\widetilde{p}}^{\star}_{i}=\frac{d\bm{p}^{\star}_{i}}{(2\pi)^{3}\sqrt{2E_{\bm{p}^{\star}_{i}}}}.

(13)

The function
\Phi_{n}(p)=(2\pi)^{3}\sqrt{2P_{n}^{0}}\delta^{3}(\bm{p}-\bm{P})

corresponds to the nucleus with a certain 3-momentum
\bm{P}
and energy
P^{0}_{n}=E_{\bm{p}}+\varepsilon_{n}
.
In formula (
13
) one has
\{p^{\star}\}\equiv(p^{\star}_{1},\dots,p^{\star}_{n})
, and

p^{\star}_{i}
is thr 4-momentum of the
i
th nucleon in the center-of-mass system of the nucleus (at rest).
After transforming the right side of (
12
), the
matrix element defined in (
11

is written as follows:

\displaystyle i\mathcal{M}_{mn}=\dfrac{iG_{\rm F}}{\sqrt{2}}\sqrt{4P^{0^{\prime}}_{m}P^{0}_{n}}\,\,l_{\mu}(k^{\prime},k,s^{\prime},s)\,h^{\mu}_{mn}(\bm{q}),

(14)

where the lepton current is denoted as

l_{\mu}(k^{\prime},k,s^{\prime},s)\equiv\overline{u}_{\chi}(\bm{k}^{\prime},s^{\prime})O_{\mu}u_{\chi}(\bm{k},s).

(15)

The hadronic current
h^{\mu}_{mn}(\bm{q})=\langle m|H^{\mu}(0)|n\rangle

defined in terms of nuclear
|n\rangle

\displaystyle h^{\mu}_{mn}(\bm{q})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum^{A}_{k}\frac{\overline{u}(\bm{\bar{p}}^{\star}_{k}+\bm{q},r^{\prime}_{k})\,O^{\mu}_{k}\,u(\bm{\bar{p}^{\star}}_{k},r_{k})}{\sqrt{4E_{\bm{\bar{p}}^{\star}_{k}}E_{\bm{\bar{p}}^{\star}_{k}+\bm{q}}}}\times

(16)

\displaystyle\times\int\prod^{A}_{i=1}\frac{d\bm{p}^{\star}_{i}\delta\big(f(\bm{p^{\star}_{k}})\big)}{(2\pi)^{3}}\widetilde{\psi}^{*}_{m}(\{p^{(k)}_{\star}\},\bm{p^{\star}}_{k}\!+\!\bm{q})\widetilde{\psi}_{n}(\{p^{\star}\})(2\pi)^{3}\delta^{3}(\sum^{A}_{i=1}\bm{p}^{\star}_{i}),

where
\bm{\bar{p}^{\star}_{k}}(\bm{q})
is the solution of the equation
1
1

1

It is assumed here that the mass of the proton
is equal to the mass of the neutron, i.e.
m_{p}=m_{n}\equiv m
.

f(\bar{\bm{p}})\equiv\sqrt{m^{2}+\bar{\bm{p}}^{2}}-\sqrt{m^{2}+(\bar{\bm{p}}+\bm{q})^{2}}-T_{A}-\Delta\varepsilon_{mn}=0.

(17)

It is the condition for the simultaneous fulfillment of the energy conservation law and the nuclear integrity
(Fig.

Figure 2:
The requirement of simultaneous fulfillment of the energy conservation law and
integrity of the nucleus ”chooses” the momentum of the active nucleon
\bar{\bm{p}}=(p_{L}(\bm{q}),p_{T})

It ”selects” the momentum of the active nucleon

\displaystyle p_{L}(\bm{q})=-\frac{|\bm{q}|}{2}\Bigg[1-\sqrt{\beta}\sqrt{1+\frac{4m_{T}^{2}}{\bm{q}^{2}(1-\beta)}}\Bigg],\text{~where~}\beta=\frac{(T_{A}+\Delta\varepsilon_{mn})^{2}}{\bm{q}^{2}},\quad m_{T}^{2}=m^{2}+p_{T}^{2}.\qquad

(18)

In formula (

\{p^{(k)}_{\star}\}
coincides with

which is equal to

As before in

\widetilde{\psi}_{n}(\{p^{\star}\})=\widetilde{\psi}_{n}(\{\bm{p}_{\star}\})\chi_{n}(\{r\}).

Here the momentum

Spin functions are normalized by the conditions

\chi_{m}^{*}(\{r\})\chi_{n}(\{r\})=\delta_{nm}\quad\text{and}\quad\chi_{n}^{*}(\{r^{\prime}\})\chi_{n}(\{r\})=\delta_{\{r^{\prime}\}\{r\}},

(20)

which mean that if (after the interaction)
all the spins of the nucleons remained unchanged, the spin state of the nucleus also did not change,
and vice versa, if the spin state of the nucleus has not changed, all the spins of the nucleons must
also remain unchanged.

Since after the interaction the spin

of this nucleon in the

\lambda^{mn}(r^{\prime},r)\equiv\lambda^{mn}(\{r^{\prime}\},\{r\})\equiv\chi^{*}_{m}(\{r^{(k)}\})\chi_{n}(\{r\})=\delta_{mn}\delta_{r^{\prime}r}+(1-\delta_{mn})\lambda^{mn}_{r^{\prime}r},

(21)

\{r^{(k)}\}\equiv\{r^{\prime}\}
is the same as
\{r\}

k

r^{\prime}_{k}
, and

Given (

from

h^{\mu}_{mn}(\bm{q})=\sum_{k=1}^{A}\frac{\bar{u}(\bar{\bm{p}}+\bm{q},r^{\prime}_{k})O^{\mu}_{k}u(\bar{\bm{p}},r_{k})}{\sqrt{4E_{\bar{\bm{p}}}E_{\bar{\bm{p}}+\bm{q}}}}\lambda^{mn}(r^{\prime},r)\ \langle m|e^{i\bm{q}\hat{\bm{X}}_{k}}|n\rangle,

\displaystyle f^{k}_{mn}(\bm{q})\equiv\langle m|e^{i\bm{q}\hat{\bm{X}}_{k}}|n\rangle\!=\!\!\!\int\!\!\prod^{A}_{i=1}\frac{d\bm{p}^{\star}_{i}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\delta\big(f(\bm{p^{\star}_{k}})\big)\widetilde{\psi}_{m}^{*}(\{\bm{p}^{(k)}_{\star}\},\bm{p^{\star}}_{k}\!+\!\bm{q})\widetilde{\psi}_{n}(\{\bm{p}_{\star}\})(2\pi)^{3}\delta^{3}\big(\sum_{l=1}^{A}\bm{p}^{\star}_{l}\big).\qquad

(23)

Here

ensures

-operator shift of the momentum of the active

Finally, for the matrix element (

(24)

In formula (

(26)

whose value is close to 1 with good accuracy, and the dependence on
n,m
and
T_{A}

manifests itself in it only at a level
O(10^{-3})

4
Cross sections of
{\chi}
particle-nucleus scattering

4.1
Coherent and incoherent contributions
to the
{\chi A\to\chi A^{(*)}}
scattering

The
observable
differential cross section of the process
\chi_{s}A\to\chi_{s^{\prime}}A^{(*)}
can be
obtained by averaging over all possible initial
|n\rangle
states and summation over all final

|m\rangle
states of the nuclear cross section (
11
).
After summation over the spin
r,r^{\prime}
indices of the (active) nucleon
at the level of

the matrix element (
24
),
the observable cross section can be written as

Bednyakov:2018mjd
;
Bednyakov:2019dbl
;
Bednyakov:2021ppn
;
Bednyakov:2022dmc

\displaystyle\frac{d\sigma_{s^{\prime}s}}{dT_{A}}(\chi A\to\chi A^{(*)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{G^{2}_{F}m_{A}}{2^{6}\pi|\bm{k}^{\chi}_{l}|^{2}m^{2}}\Big[T^{s^{\prime}s}_{m=n}+T^{s^{\prime}s}_{m\neq n}\Big],\quad\text{where}

(27)

\displaystyle T^{s^{\prime}s}_{m=n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g_{\text{c}}\sum^{A}_{k,j}\sum_{n}\omega_{n}\Big[f^{k}_{nn}f^{j*}_{nn}\sum_{r}(l_{s^{\prime}s},h^{k}_{rr})\sum_{x}(l_{s^{\prime}s}\,h^{j}_{xx})^{*}\Big],

(28)

\displaystyle T^{s^{\prime}s}_{m\neq n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g_{\text{i}}\sum^{A}_{k,j}\sum_{n}\omega_{n}\Big[\sum_{m\neq n}f^{k}_{mn}f^{j*}_{mn}\sum_{r^{\prime}r}\lambda^{mn}_{r^{\prime}r}(l_{s^{\prime}s},h^{k}_{r^{\prime}r})\Big(\sum_{x^{\prime}x}\lambda^{mn}_{x^{\prime}x}(l_{s^{\prime}s}\,h^{j}_{x^{\prime}x})\Big)^{{\dagger}}\Big].

(29)

Here
\sum_{n}\omega_{n}=1
is the probability sum of all possible initial states of nucleus
A
.
For the sake of completeness, the correction kinematic coefficients

g_{\text{c}}=C_{1,nn}C_{nn}
and
g_{\text{i}}=C_{1,mn}C_{mn}
are kept,
whose values are close to 1 at the
O(10^{-3})
level.

Summation over index the
n
in formula
(
28
)
determines the nucleon form factors averaged over all possible initial states of the nucleus

Bednyakov:2018mjd
;
Bednyakov:2019dbl
;
Bednyakov:2021ppn
;
Bednyakov:2022dmc

\sum_{n}\omega_{n}f_{nn}^{k}f_{nn}^{j*}=\left\{\begin{matrix}|F_{p/n}(\bm{q})|^{2},&~~~~~~~~~~~~\text{ when }(k,j)=(p,p)\text{~~or~}(n,n);\\
F_{p}(\bm{q})F_{n}^{*}(\bm{q}),&\text{ when }(k,j)=(p,n);\\
F_{n}(\bm{q})F_{p}^{*}(\bm{q}),&\text{ when }(k,j)=(n,p).\\
\end{matrix}\right.

(30)

With this definition in mind, expression
(
28
)
for the contribution to the cross section corresponding to the preservation of the initial state of the nucleus,
when the projection of the spin of the active nucleon does not change,
can be represented as the square of the modulus of the sum of the contributions of protons and neutrons

\displaystyle T^{s^{\prime}s}_{m=n}(\bm{q})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g_{\text{c}}\Big|\sum_{f=p,n}\sum^{A_{f}}_{k=1}\sum_{r}(l_{s^{\prime}s},h^{f}_{rr}(\bm{q}))F_{f}(\bm{q})\Big|^{2}.

(31)

Here
A_{f}
denotes the total number of
f
-type nucleons in the nucleus.

To sum over the nuclear indices
m,n
in formula
(
29
),
note that from the spin function normalization (
20
),
for
the same

k
th nucleon there follows the relation

\lambda^{mn}_{r^{\prime}_{k}r_{k}}[\lambda^{mn}_{x^{\prime}_{k}x_{k}}]^{*}\equiv\delta_{r^{\prime}_{k}x^{\prime}_{k}}\delta_{r_{k}x_{k}}|\lambda^{mn}_{r^{\prime}_{k}r_{k}}|^{2},\quad\text{where }\quad|\lambda^{mn}_{r^{\prime}_{k}r_{k}}|^{2}=1.

(32)

Therefore, one can assume that
\lambda^{mn}_{r^{\prime}r}
do not depend on indices
m
and
n
;
however,
\lambda^{mn}_{r^{\prime}r}\simeq\lambda^{p/n}_{r^{\prime}r}
may differ for protons and neutrons.
In other words, for any initial spin orientation of the active nucleon (index
r
)
any orientation of the spin of this nucleon (index
r^{\prime}
) is possible after the nuclear
|n\rangle\to|m\rangle
transition
3
3

3

Discussion of this approximation is given in Appendix
6
.
. As a result,
\lambda^{mn}_{r^{\prime}r}
can be taken out of the summation over
m,n
in
(
29
).
Then, if the indices
k
and
j
in formula
(
29
)
“indicate” the same nucleon, e.g., the proton, the summation gives

\displaystyle\sum_{n}\omega_{n}\sum_{m\neq n}f_{mn}^{k}f_{mn}^{k*}=\sum_{n}\omega_{n}\Big[\langle n|e^{i\bm{q}\bm{X}_{k}}\sum_{m}|m\rangle\langle m|e^{-i\bm{q}\bm{X}_{k}}|n\rangle\Big]-|F_{p}(\bm{q})|^{2}=1-|F_{p}(\bm{q})|^{2}.\qquad

(33)

If
k\neq j
, but they still indicate protons (index
p
), it can be written that

\sum_{n}\omega_{n}\sum_{m\neq n}f_{mn}^{k}f_{mn}^{j*}=\sum_{n}\omega_{n}\text{cov}_{nn}(e^{i\bm{q}\hat{\bm{X}}_{k}},e^{-i\bm{q}\hat{\bm{X}}_{j}})\equiv\langle\text{cov}(e^{i\bm{q}\hat{\bm{X}}_{k}},e^{-i\bm{q}\hat{\bm{X}}_{j}})\rangle_{p},

(34)

where the covariation operator of the shift operators
e^{-i\bm{q}\hat{\bm{X}}_{j}}

and
e^{i\bm{q}\hat{\bm{X}}_{k}}
with respect to the state
|n\rangle
is introduced in the form

\text{cov}_{nn}(e^{i\bm{q}\hat{\bm{X}}_{k}},e^{-i\bm{q}\hat{\bm{X}}_{j}})\equiv\langle n|e^{i\bm{q}\hat{\bm{X}}_{k}}\,e^{-i\bm{q}\hat{\bm{X}_{j}}}|n\rangle-\langle n|e^{i\bm{q}\hat{\bm{X}}_{k}}|n\rangle\langle n|e^{-i\bm{q}\hat{\bm{X}}_{j}}|n\rangle.

(35)

Expression (
34
)
vanishes at small and at large transfer momenta.
A similar consideration applies to neutrons and can be generalized to the common case of protons and neutrons.
It is believed that when correlations between nucleons in the nucleus are sufficiently weak,
one can neglect the covariance functions like (
34
).
For example, in nuclear shell models where multiparticle wave functions of nuclei
are constructed in the form of a product of single-particle
wave functions
Blokhintsev:1963
;
Bohr:1974
, covariation (
35
)
identically vanishes.
Therefore, in what follows as in
Bednyakov:2018mjd
;
Bednyakov:2019dbl
;
Bednyakov:2021ppn
;
Bednyakov:2022dmc
,
covariance contributions like (
34
)
are
completely neglected
.
In view of the above the term
(
29
)
can be finally presented as a sum over all nucleons

\displaystyle T^{s^{\prime}s}_{m\neq n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g_{\text{i}}\sum_{f=p,n}\big[1-|F_{f}(\bm{q})|^{2}\big]\sum^{A_{f}}_{k=1}\sum_{r^{\prime}r}\big|(l_{s^{\prime}s}\,h^{f}_{r^{\prime}r}(\bm{q}))\big|^{2}.

(36)

Thus, the observable differential cross section
(
27
)
for the process
\chi A\to\chi A^{(*)}
can be written in the form
of two fundamentally different terms

\displaystyle\frac{d\sigma_{s^{\prime}s}}{dT_{A}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{d\sigma^{s^{\prime}s}_{\text{coh}}}{dT_{A}}(\chi A\to\chi A)+\frac{d\sigma^{s^{\prime}s}_{\text{inc}}}{dT_{A}}(\chi A\to\chi A^{*}),\quad\text{where }

(37)

\displaystyle\frac{d\sigma^{s^{\prime}s}_{\text{coh}}}{dT_{A}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c_{A}g_{\text{c}}\Big|\sum_{f=n,p}\sum_{k=1}^{A_{f}}\sum_{r}(l_{s^{\prime}s}\,h^{f}_{rr}(\bm{q}))F_{f}(\bm{q})\Big|^{2}\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\frac{d\sigma^{s^{\prime}s}_{\text{inc}}}{dT_{A}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c_{A}g_{\text{i}}\!\sum_{f=n,p}\sum_{k=1}^{A_{f}}\sum_{r^{\prime}r}|(l_{s^{\prime}s}\,h^{f}_{r^{\prime}r}(\bm{q}))|^{2}[1-|F_{f}(\bm{q})|^{2}],

respectively correspond to the elastic and inelastic interactions of the
\chi
particle with nucleus
A
.
For convenience, in formulas (
37
)
universal factors are introduced,

c_{A}\equiv\dfrac{G^{2}_{F}m_{A}}{2^{6}\pi|\bm{k}^{\chi}_{l}|^{2}m^{2}}\quad\text{and}\quad\hat{c}_{A}\equiv\dfrac{G^{2}_{F}m_{A}}{4\pi}\dfrac{1}{|\bm{k}^{\chi}_{l}|^{2}m^{2}}=4^{2}c_{A},

(38)

where
\bm{k}^{\chi}_{l}
is the momentum of the incident
\chi
particle in the rest frame of the target nucleus.
The helicities of the
\chi
particle (in the general case, the projection of the spin onto some direction) in the initial

s
and final
s^{\prime}
states are assumed to be fixed in
(
37
).
In the future, they can be averaged (summated).

Since the scalar products
(l_{s^{\prime}s}\,h^{f}_{r^{\prime}r})

in formulas (
37
)
depend only on the type of the active nucleon and do not depend on its number
(that is, from the summation index
k
), then the summation over
k

in formulas (
37
)
gives the total number of protons and neutrons in the nucleus.
As a result, the differential cross sections for

\chi
particle scattering on the nucleus
\chi_{s}A\to\chi_{s^{\prime}}A^{(*)}
take the form

\displaystyle\frac{d\sigma^{s^{\prime}s}_{\text{inc}}}{dT_{A}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c_{A}g_{\text{i}}\sum_{f=p,n}[1-|F_{f}(\bm{q})|^{2}]\Big[A^{f}_{+}\sum_{r^{\prime}=\pm}|(l_{s^{\prime}s}\,h^{\eta,f}_{r^{\prime}+})|^{2}+A^{f}_{-}\sum_{r^{\prime}=\pm}|(l_{s^{\prime}s}\,h^{\eta,f}_{r^{\prime}-})|^{2}\Big],\qquad

\displaystyle\frac{d\sigma^{s^{\prime}s}_{\text{coh}}}{dT_{A}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c_{A}g_{\text{c}}\Big|\sum_{f=p,n}F_{f}(\bm{q})[A^{f}_{+}(l_{s^{\prime}s}\,h^{\eta,f}_{++})+A^{f}_{-}(l_{s^{\prime}s}\,h^{\eta,f}_{--})]\Big|^{2}.

(39)

Here the sums over the initial projections of the nucleon spin on the direction of arrival of the
\chi
particle,
which is marked by the index
\eta
for hadronic currents, are explicitly distinguished.
In (
4.1
)

A^{f}_{\pm}
is a number of
f
-type nucleons with the spin projection
\pm 1
onto this direction.

It is convenient to
finally

rewrite formulas (
4.1
)
in terms of the total number of
f
-type nucleons,

A_{f}=A^{f}_{+}+A^{f}_{-}
, and the difference in the number of nucleons,
\Delta A_{f}=A^{f}_{+}-A^{f}_{-}
, having positive and negative spin projections on the selected direction

\displaystyle\frac{d\sigma^{s^{\prime}s}_{\text{coh}}(\bm{q})}{g_{\text{c}}dT_{A}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c_{A}\Big|\sum_{f=p,n}F_{f}(\bm{q})\dfrac{A_{f}}{2}Q_{f}^{s^{\prime}s}\Big|^{2},

(40)

\displaystyle\frac{d\sigma^{s^{\prime}s}_{\text{inc}}(\bm{q})}{g_{\text{i}}dT_{A}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c_{A}\sum_{f=p,n}\widehat{F}^{2}_{f}(\bm{q})\dfrac{A_{f}}{2}\Big[\,Q^{s^{\prime}s}_{+}+\dfrac{\Delta A_{f}}{A_{f}}\,Q^{s^{\prime}s}_{-}\Big],\quad\text{where}\quad\widehat{F}^{2}_{f}(\bm{q})\equiv\big[1-F^{2}_{f}(\bm{q})\big].

Thus, coherent and incoherent cross sections are defined, respectively, by the following combinations of scalar products:

Q_{f}^{s^{\prime}s}\equiv\hat{Q}^{s^{\prime}s}_{+}+\dfrac{\Delta A_{f}}{A_{f}}\hat{Q}^{s^{\prime}s}_{-},\quad\text{where}\quad\hat{Q}^{s^{\prime}s}_{\pm}\equiv(l_{s^{\prime}s}\,h^{f}_{++})\pm(l_{s^{\prime}s}\,h^{f}_{--}),

(41)

\text{and}\quad Q^{s^{\prime}s}_{\pm}\equiv\sum_{r^{\prime}=\pm}|(l_{s^{\prime}s}\,h^{f}_{r^{\prime}+})|^{2}\pm\sum_{r^{\prime}=\pm}|(l_{s^{\prime}s}\,h^{f}_{r^{\prime}-})|^{2}.

(42)

As a rule, if a nucleus has spin zero, then
\Delta A_{f}=0
.
Almost always
{\Delta A_{f}}\ll{A_{f}}
, except for such nuclei as
helium
Lyon:2022sza
, which, perhaps, deserves special attention.
To complete the derivation of the differential cross section of
the process
\chi A\to\chi A^{(*)}
,
it is necessary to have explicit expressions for scalar products
(l_{s^{\prime}s}\,h^{p/n}_{r^{\prime}r})
.
These values are given in section
4.2
.

4.2
Scalar products for
{\chi A\to\chi A^{(*)}}
scattering

Scalar products of
\chi
-lepton and nucleon currents obtained in
Bednyakov:2021pgs

are necessary for self-consistent calculations of coherent and incoherent
\chi A\to\chi A^{(*)}

scattering cross sections.
They are defined by the expression

(l^{i}_{s^{\prime}s}\,h^{k}_{r^{\prime}r})\equiv\sum^{4}_{\mu,\nu}J^{i,\mu}_{s^{\prime}s}(\bm{k}^{\prime})\,g_{\mu\nu}\,J^{k,\nu}_{r^{\prime}r}(\bm{p}^{\prime}),

where indices
s
and
r
denote the initial spin states of the lepton and the nucleon,
and the indices
s^{\prime}
and
r^{\prime}
denote their final states.
The indices
i
and
k
denote the vector (
J^{v,\mu}\equiv V^{\mu}
), axial vector (
J^{a,\mu}\equiv A^{\mu}
)
lepton (argument
\bm{k}^{\prime}
) and nucleon (argument
\bm{p}^{\prime}
) currents.
The scalar products are expressed in term of the masses of the nucleon and the
\chi
particle

m,\quad m_{\chi},\quad\text{and parameters}\quad\lambda_{\pm}=\sqrt{E_{p}\pm m},\qquad\xi_{\pm}=\sqrt{E_{\chi}\pm m_{\chi}},

as well as the scattering angle
\theta
of the
\chi
particle in the lepton and nucleon center-of-mass system (c.m.s.),
where
\bm{k}+\bm{p}=\bm{k}^{\prime}+\bm{p}^{\prime}=0
.
Here
p
and
k
are the 4-momenta of the nucleon and
\chi
particles, in addition,

\displaystyle E_{\chi}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\sqrt{m^{2}_{\chi}+|\bm{k}|^{2}}=\sqrt{m^{2}_{\chi}+|\bm{k}^{\prime}|^{2}}=\frac{s+m^{2}_{\chi}-m^{2}}{2\sqrt{s}},

\displaystyle E_{p}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\sqrt{m^{2}+|\bm{p}|^{2}}=\sqrt{m^{2}+|\bm{p}^{\prime}|^{2}}=\frac{s+m^{2}-m^{2}_{\chi}}{2\sqrt{s}},

(43)

\displaystyle|\bm{p}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{E^{2}_{p}-m^{2}}=\lambda_{+}\lambda_{-}=|\bm{k}|=\sqrt{E^{2}_{\chi}-m^{2}_{\chi}}=\xi_{+}\xi_{-}=\dfrac{\lambda(s,m^{2},m^{2}_{\chi})}{2\sqrt{s}},\text{~~where}

\displaystyle\lambda^{2}(s,m^{2},m^{2}_{\chi})\equiv[s-(m+m_{\chi})^{2}][s-(m-m_{\chi})^{2}].

The invariant square of the total energy in the c.m.s. has the form

\displaystyle s_{\text{c.s.m.}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(p+k)^{2}=(p_{0}+k_{0})^{2}-(\bm{p}+\bm{k})^{2}=(E_{\chi}+E_{p})^{2}.

In this case, the momenta of the particles do not change:
|\bm{k}^{\prime}|=|\bm{k}|=|\bm{p}^{\prime}|=|\bm{p}|
.
Therefore, the only free variable of
\chi
-lepton elastic scattering on the nucleon in c.m.s. takes place.
It is the angle
\theta
between the direction of the initial lepton momentum
\bm{k}
and the direction of its final
momentum
\bm{k}^{\prime}
. It is present in the 3-momentum transferred to the nucleon

\bm{q}^{2}=2|\bm{k}|^{2}(1-\cos\theta)\equiv\bm{q}^{2}_{\max}\sin^{2}\dfrac{\theta}{2}.

(44)

In the lab frame, where the nucleon is at rest,
p_{l}=(m,\bm{p}_{l}=\bm{0})
, one has

s=(k_{l}+p_{l})^{2}=m^{2}_{\chi}+m^{2}+2m\sqrt{m^{2}_{\chi}+[\bm{k}^{l}_{\chi}]^{2}}=m^{2}_{\chi}+m^{2}+2mm_{\chi}\Big[1+\dfrac{2T_{0}}{m_{\chi}}\Big]^{1/2},

(45)

where
T_{0}=\dfrac{|\bm{k}^{l}_{\chi}|^{2}}{2m_{\chi}}
is the kinetic energy of the lepton incident on the nucleon at rest.
The following relation is also useful:

\frac{|\bm{k}|^{2}}{|\bm{k}^{l}_{\chi}|^{2}}=\frac{m^{2}}{s}.

(46)

Considering the definition of the angle
\theta
in terms of the recoil energy of the nucleus
T_{A}
4
4

4

Since
\bm{q}^{2}\ll m_{A}^{2}
, then
\bm{q}^{2}\simeq 2m_{A}T_{A}
, and from (
3
) follows
T_{A}^{\max}\simeq m_{A}\Big(1+\frac{4|\bm{k}^{l}_{\chi}|^{2}}{2m^{2}_{A}}\Big)-m_{A}=\dfrac{4m_{\chi}T_{0}}{m_{A}}
.

\sin^{2}\dfrac{\theta}{2}=\dfrac{\bm{q}^{2}}{\bm{q}^{2}_{\max}}\simeq\dfrac{T_{A}}{T_{A}^{\max}},\quad\text{where}\quad T_{A}^{\max}\simeq\dfrac{4m_{\chi}T_{0}}{m_{A}},

(47)

the parameters
T_{0}
(the initial kinetic energy of the
\chi
particle) through relations
(
4.2
), and (
45
)
and
T_{A}
(the kinetic energy of the recoil of the nucleus) determine
all quantities necessary for calculating the cross sections.

Recall in this regard that according to the condition of preserving the integrity of the nucleus
(
17
),
in the rest frame of the nucleus

the active nucleon ”meets” the
\chi
particle incident on the nucleus
with a nonzero momentum from
(
18
),
and therefore the
s
invariant in the rest frame of the nucleus (lab frame) should be recalculated

\displaystyle s

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(k_{l}+p_{l})^{2}=m^{2}_{\chi}+m^{2}+2mm_{\chi}\Bigg\{\sqrt{1+\dfrac{|\bm{k}^{l}_{\chi}|^{2}}{m_{\chi}^{2}}}\sqrt{1+\dfrac{p_{L}^{2}}{m^{2}}}-\dfrac{|\bm{k}^{l}_{\chi}|}{m_{\chi}}\dfrac{p_{L}}{m}\Bigg\}.

(48)

In other words, this value depends both on the kinetic energy of the incident
\chi
particle
T_{0}
(or
|\bm{k}^{l}_{\chi}|
) and on the kinetic energy of the recoil of the nucleus
T_{A}
.
However, as a rule, one has
{p_{L}}\leq 0.1\,{m}
, and this correction is small enough.

The scalar products of the lepton and nucleon currents with
the spin projection of the nucleon (
r^{\prime},r=\pm 1
) and the
\chi
-particle (
s^{\prime},s=\pm 1
),
given below are defined as follows

Bednyakov:2021pgs
:

\displaystyle(l^{w}_{s^{\prime}s}\,h^{w,f}_{r^{\prime}r})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\alpha_{f}(l^{v}_{s^{\prime}s}\,h^{v}_{r^{\prime}r})+\beta_{f}(l^{v}_{s^{\prime}s}\,h^{a}_{r^{\prime}r})+\gamma_{f}(l^{a}_{s^{\prime}s}\,h^{v}_{r^{\prime}r})+\delta_{f}(l^{a}_{s^{\prime}s}\,h^{a}_{r^{\prime}r}).

(49)

Recall that the index
f
denotes a neutron or a proton.

When the
\chi
-lepton helicity and the nucleon spin projection are preserved,
these scalar products have the form

\displaystyle(l^{w}_{\pm\pm}\,h^{w,f}_{\pm\pm})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\Big[(\alpha_{f}-\delta_{f})\big[\xi_{+}\xi_{-}\lambda_{+}\lambda_{-}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+(m_{\chi}+\xi^{2}_{-})(m+\lambda^{2}_{-}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2})\big]

\displaystyle\pm\,\lambda_{+}\lambda_{-}(\gamma_{f}-\beta_{f})\big[(m_{\chi}+\xi^{2}_{-})\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+m+\lambda^{2}_{-}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\big]\Big],

\displaystyle(l^{w}_{\pm\pm}\,h^{w,f}_{\mp\mp})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\Big[(\alpha_{f}+\delta_{f})(m_{\chi}+\xi^{2}_{-})m+\xi_{+}\xi_{-}\lambda_{+}\lambda_{-}\big(\alpha_{f}+\alpha_{f}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+\delta_{f}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\big)+

\displaystyle+(m_{\chi}+\xi^{2}_{-})\lambda^{2}_{-}\big(\delta_{f}+\delta_{f}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+\alpha_{f}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\big)\pm\lambda_{+}\lambda_{-}\big\{(\beta_{f}+\gamma_{f})m

\displaystyle+\lambda^{2}_{-}\big(\beta_{f}+\beta_{f}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+\gamma_{f}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\big)+(m_{\chi}+\xi^{2}_{-})\big(\gamma_{f}+\gamma_{f}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+\beta_{f}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\big)\big\}\Big].

When the helicity of the
\chi
lepton is preserved, but the projection of the nucleon spin changes:

\displaystyle(l^{w}_{\pm\pm}\,h^{w,f}_{\pm\mp})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mp 4\sin\frac{\theta}{2}e^{\mp i\phi}\Big[2m\delta_{f}(m_{\chi}+\xi^{2}_{-})+\xi_{+}\xi_{-}\lambda_{+}\lambda_{-}(\alpha_{f}+\alpha_{f}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+\delta_{f}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2})

\displaystyle+(\xi^{2}_{-}+m_{\chi})\lambda^{2}_{-}(\delta_{f}+\delta_{f}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+\alpha_{f}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2})\pm\lambda_{+}\lambda_{-}\big\{2m\beta_{f}+

\displaystyle+(\xi^{2}_{-}+m_{\chi})(\gamma_{f}+\gamma_{f}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+\beta_{f}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2})+\lambda^{2}_{-}(\beta_{f}+\beta_{f}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+\gamma_{f}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2})\big\}\Big],

\displaystyle(l^{w}_{\pm\pm}\,h^{w,f}_{\mp\pm})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}e^{\pm i\phi}\Big[(\gamma_{f}-\beta_{f})\lambda_{+}\lambda_{-}[m_{\chi}+\lambda^{2}_{-}+\xi^{2}_{-}]

\displaystyle\pm(\alpha_{f}-\delta_{f})[\xi_{+}\xi_{-}\lambda_{+}\lambda_{-}+(\xi^{2}_{-}+m_{\chi})\lambda^{2}_{-}]\Big].

When the helicity of the
\chi
lepton changes, but the projection of the nucleon spin is preserved:

\displaystyle(l^{w}_{\pm\mp}\,h^{w,f}_{\mp\mp})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4m_{\chi}\sin\frac{\theta}{2}e^{\mp i\phi}\Big[(\beta_{f}-\gamma_{f})\lambda_{+}\lambda_{-}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\pm(\alpha_{f}-\delta_{f})(m+\lambda^{2}_{-}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2})\Big],

\displaystyle(l^{w}_{\pm\mp}\,h^{w,f}_{\pm\pm})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4m_{\chi}\sin\frac{\theta}{2}e^{\mp i\phi}\Big[(\gamma_{f}-\beta_{f})\lambda_{+}\lambda_{-}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\pm\big\{(\alpha_{f}+\delta_{f})m+(\alpha_{f}-\delta_{f})\lambda^{2}_{-}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\big\}\Big].

Finally, when both the helicity of the
\chi
lepton and the projection of the nucleon spin change:

\displaystyle(l^{w}_{\pm\mp}\,h^{w,f}_{\mp\pm})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4m_{\chi}\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\Big[(\alpha_{f}-\delta_{f})\lambda^{2}_{-}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}-2\delta_{f}m\pm(\gamma_{f}-\beta_{f})\lambda_{+}\lambda_{-}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\Big],

\displaystyle(l^{w}_{\pm\mp}\,h^{w,f}_{\pm\mp})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-4m_{\chi}\lambda_{-}\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}e^{\mp 2i\phi}\big[(\alpha_{f}-\delta_{f})\lambda_{-}\mp(\gamma_{f}-\beta_{f})\lambda_{+}\big].

It is convenient to have scalar products in the form of an expansion in the coupling constants of weak currents

\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta
(the index
f
is temporarily omitted) in the following compact form

\displaystyle\frac{(l^{w}_{\pm\pm}\,h^{w,f}_{\pm\pm})}{4\cos\frac{\theta}{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\alpha{f_{1}(\theta)}-\delta{f_{1}(\theta)}\pm\gamma{f_{2}(\theta)}\mp\beta{f_{2}(\theta)},

\displaystyle\frac{(l^{w}_{\pm\pm}\,h^{w,f}_{\mp\mp})}{4\cos\frac{\theta}{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\alpha{f_{3}(\theta)}+\delta{f_{4}(\theta)}\pm\beta{f_{5}(\theta)}\pm\gamma{f_{6}(\theta)},

\displaystyle\frac{(l^{w}_{\pm\pm}\,h^{w,f}_{\pm\mp})}{\mp 4\sin\frac{\theta}{2}e^{\mp i\phi}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\alpha{f_{7}(\theta)}+\delta{f_{8}(\theta)}\pm\beta{f_{9}(\theta)}\pm\gamma{f_{10}(\theta)},

\displaystyle\frac{(l^{w}_{\pm\pm}\,h^{w,f}_{\mp\pm})}{4\sin\frac{\theta}{2}e^{\pm i\phi}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pm\alpha{f_{11}(\theta)}\mp\delta{f_{11}(\theta)}+\gamma{f_{12}(\theta)}-\beta{f_{12}(\theta)};

\displaystyle\frac{(l^{w}_{\pm\mp}\,h^{w,f}_{\mp\mp})}{4\sin\frac{\theta}{2}e^{\mp i\phi}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pm\alpha{f_{13}(\theta)}\mp\delta{f_{13}(\theta)}+\beta{f_{14}(\theta)}-\gamma{f_{14}(\theta)},

(50)

\displaystyle\frac{(l^{w}_{\pm\mp}\,h^{w,f}_{\pm\pm})}{4\sin\frac{\theta}{2}e^{\mp i\phi}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pm\alpha{f_{13}(\theta)}\pm\delta{f_{15}(\theta)}+\gamma{f_{14}(\theta)}-\beta{f_{14}(\theta)},

\displaystyle\frac{(l^{w}_{\pm\mp}\,h^{w,f}_{\mp\pm})}{4\cos\frac{\theta}{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\alpha{f_{16}(\theta)}-\delta{f_{17}(\theta)}\pm\gamma_{f}{f_{14}(\theta)}\mp\beta_{f}{f_{14}(\theta)},

\displaystyle\frac{(l^{w}_{\pm\mp}\,h^{w,f}_{\pm\mp})}{4\cos\frac{\theta}{2}e^{\mp 2i\phi}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\alpha{f_{16}(\theta)}+\delta{f_{16}(\theta)}\pm\gamma{f_{14}(\theta)}\mp\beta{f_{14}(\theta)}.

The following ”form-factor-type” notations are introduced here:

\displaystyle f_{1}(\theta)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle E_{\chi}m+(E_{\chi}+\lambda_{+}^{2})\lambda^{2}_{-}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2},\qquad f_{2}(\theta)\equiv\lambda_{+}\lambda_{-}\big[m+(E_{\chi}+\lambda^{2}_{-})\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\big],

\displaystyle f_{3}(\theta)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle E_{\chi}(m+\lambda^{2}_{-}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2})+\lambda^{2}_{+}\lambda^{2}_{-}(1+\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}),

\displaystyle f_{4}(\theta)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle E_{\chi}m+E_{\chi}\lambda^{2}_{-}(1+\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2})+\lambda^{2}_{+}\lambda^{2}_{-}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2},

\displaystyle f_{5}(\theta)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\lambda_{+}\lambda_{-}[m+\lambda^{2}_{-}(1+\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2})+E_{\chi}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}],

\displaystyle f_{6}(\theta)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\lambda_{+}\lambda_{-}[m+\lambda^{2}_{-}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+E_{\chi}(1+\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2})],

\displaystyle f_{7}(\theta)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\lambda^{2}_{+}\lambda^{2}_{-}(1+\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2})+E_{\chi}\lambda^{2}_{-}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2},

(51)

\displaystyle f_{8}(\theta)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle 2E_{\chi}m+\lambda^{2}_{+}\lambda^{2}_{-}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+E_{\chi}\lambda^{2}_{-}(1+\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}),

\displaystyle f_{9}(\theta)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\lambda_{+}\lambda_{-}[2m+E_{\chi}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+\lambda^{2}_{-}(1+\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2})]

\displaystyle f_{10}(\theta)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\lambda_{+}\lambda_{-}[E_{\chi}(1+\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2})+\lambda^{2}_{-}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}],\qquad f_{11}(\theta)\equiv(E_{\chi}+\lambda^{2}_{+})\lambda^{2}_{-}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2},

\displaystyle f_{12}(\theta)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\lambda_{+}\lambda_{-}(E_{\chi}+\lambda^{2}_{-})\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2},\qquad\qquad\qquad f_{13}(\theta)\equiv m_{\chi}(m+\lambda^{2}_{-}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}),

\displaystyle f_{14}(\theta)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle m_{\chi}\lambda_{+}\lambda_{-}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2},\quad\qquad f_{15}(\theta)\equiv m_{\chi}(m-\lambda^{2}_{-}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2})=f_{13}(\theta)-2m_{\chi}\lambda^{2}_{-}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2},

\displaystyle f_{16}(\theta)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle m_{\chi}\lambda^{2}_{-}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2},\qquad\qquad f_{17}(\theta)\equiv m_{\chi}(2m+\lambda^{2}_{-}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2})=2m_{\chi}m+f_{16}(\theta).

The squares of these scalar products are written as follows:

\displaystyle\frac{|(l^{w}_{\pm\pm}\,h^{w,f}_{\pm\pm})|^{2}}{16\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\alpha^{2}+\delta^{2})f_{1}^{2}-2\alpha\delta f_{1}^{2}+(\beta^{2}+\gamma^{2})f_{2}^{2}-2\beta\gamma f_{2}^{2}\mp 2(\alpha\beta+\gamma\delta)f_{1}f_{2}\pm 2(\alpha\gamma+\beta\delta)f_{1}f_{2},

\displaystyle\frac{|(l^{w}_{\pm\pm}\,h^{w,f}_{\mp\mp})|^{2}}{16\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\alpha^{2}f_{3}(\theta)^{2}+\beta^{2}f_{5}(\theta)^{2}+\gamma^{2}f_{6}(\theta)^{2}+\delta^{2}f_{4}(\theta)^{2}+2\alpha\delta f_{3}(\theta)f_{4}(\theta)+2\beta\gamma f_{5}(\theta)f_{6}(\theta)

\displaystyle\pm 2\alpha\beta f_{3}(\theta)f_{5}(\theta)\pm 2\alpha\gamma f_{3}(\theta)f_{6}(\theta)\pm 2\beta\delta f_{4}(\theta)f_{5}(\theta)\pm 2\gamma\delta f_{4}(\theta)f_{6}(\theta),

\displaystyle\frac{|(l^{w}_{\pm\pm}\,h^{w,f}_{\pm\mp})|^{2}}{16\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\alpha^{2}f_{7}(\theta)^{2}+\delta^{2}f_{8}(\theta)^{2}+2\alpha\delta f_{7}(\theta)f_{8}(\theta)+\beta^{2}f_{9}(\theta)^{2}+\gamma^{2}f_{10}(\theta)^{2}+2\beta\gamma f_{9}(\theta)f_{10}(\theta)

\displaystyle\pm 2\alpha\beta f_{7}(\theta)f_{9}(\theta)\pm 2\alpha\gamma f_{7}(\theta)f_{10}(\theta)\pm 2\beta\delta f_{9}(\theta)f_{8}(\theta)\pm 2\gamma\delta f_{8}(\theta)f_{10}(\theta),

\displaystyle\frac{|(l^{w}_{\pm\pm}\,h^{w,f}_{\mp\pm})|^{2}}{16\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\alpha^{2}+\delta^{2}-2\alpha\delta)f_{11}(\theta)^{2}+(\beta^{2}+\gamma^{2}-2\beta\gamma)f_{12}(\theta)^{2}

\displaystyle\mp 2(\alpha\beta+\gamma\delta)f_{11}(\theta)f_{12}(\theta)\pm 2(\alpha\gamma+\beta\delta)f_{11}(\theta)f_{12}(\theta);

\displaystyle\frac{|(l^{w}_{\pm\mp}\,h^{w,f}_{\mp\mp})|^{2}}{16\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\alpha^{2}+\delta^{2}-2\alpha\delta)f_{13}(\theta)^{2}+(\beta^{2}+\gamma^{2}-2\beta\gamma)f_{14}(\theta)^{2}

\displaystyle\pm 2(\alpha\beta+\gamma\delta)f_{13}(\theta)f_{14}(\theta)\mp 2(\alpha\gamma+\beta\delta)f_{13}(\theta)f_{14}(\theta),

\displaystyle\frac{|(l^{w}_{\pm\mp}\,h^{w,f}_{\pm\pm})|^{2}}{16\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\alpha^{2}f_{13}(\theta)^{2}+\delta^{2}f_{15}(\theta)^{2}+2\alpha\delta f_{13}(\theta)f_{15}(\theta)+(\beta^{2}+\gamma^{2}-2\beta\gamma)f_{14}(\theta)^{2}

\displaystyle\mp 2(\alpha\beta-\alpha\gamma)f_{13}(\theta)f_{14}(\theta)\mp 2(\beta\delta-\gamma\delta)f_{14}(\theta)f_{15}(\theta),

\displaystyle\frac{|(l^{w}_{\pm\mp}\,h^{w,f}_{\mp\pm})|^{2}}{16\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\alpha^{2}f_{16}(\theta)^{2}+\delta^{2}f_{17}(\theta)^{2}-2\alpha\delta f_{16}(\theta)f_{17}(\theta)+(\beta^{2}+\gamma^{2}-2\beta\gamma)f_{14}(\theta)^{2}

\displaystyle\pm 2\alpha(\gamma-\beta)f_{14}(\theta)f_{16}(\theta)\pm 2\delta(\beta-\gamma)f_{14}(\theta)f_{17}(\theta),

\displaystyle\frac{|(l^{w}_{\pm\mp}\,h^{w,f}_{\pm\mp})|^{2}}{16\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\alpha^{2}+\delta^{2}-2\alpha\delta)f_{16}(\theta)^{2}+(\beta^{2}+\gamma^{2}-2\beta\gamma)f_{14}(\theta)^{2}

\displaystyle\pm 2(\alpha\beta+\gamma\delta)f_{14}(\theta)f_{16}(\theta)\mp 2(\alpha\gamma+\beta\delta)f_{14}(\theta)f_{16}(\theta).

These scalar products of the nucleon and lepton currents are the basis for calculation of coherent (elastic) and incoherent (inelastic) cross sections for the
\chi
particle-nucleus interaction in the relativistic approximation.

4.3
Relativistic cross sections of the process
{\chi A\to\chi A^{(*)}}

Coherent cross sections.

According to definition (
40
), the
coherent
\chi A
cross sections are given by
\hat{Q}^{s^{\prime}s}_{\pm}
combinations of scalar products
(
41
), which look like

\displaystyle\frac{\hat{Q}^{\mp\mp}_{+}}{8\cos\frac{\theta}{2}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\alpha f_{\alpha+}(\theta)+\delta f_{\delta-}(\theta)\mp\beta f_{\beta-}(\theta)\mp\gamma f_{\gamma+}(\theta),

\displaystyle\frac{\hat{Q}^{\mp\mp}_{-}}{8\cos\frac{\theta}{2}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\pm[\alpha f_{\alpha-}(\theta)+\delta f_{\delta+}(\theta)\mp\beta f_{\beta+}(\theta)\mp\gamma f_{\gamma-}(\theta)];

\displaystyle\frac{\hat{Q}^{\mp\pm}_{+}}{8\sin\frac{\theta}{2}e^{\pm i\phi}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mp\alpha\hat{f}_{\alpha}(\theta)\pm\delta\hat{f}_{\delta-}(\theta),\qquad\frac{\hat{Q}^{\mp\pm}_{-}}{8\sin\frac{\theta}{2}e^{\pm i\phi}}=\mp(\gamma-\beta)\hat{f}_{\beta\gamma}(\theta)+\delta\hat{f}_{\delta+}(\theta).

Then the
\displaystyle Q_{f}^{s^{\prime}s}
values from (
41
) are

\displaystyle\frac{Q_{f}^{\mp\mp}}{8\cos\frac{\theta}{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\alpha\big[f_{\alpha+}(\theta)\pm\dfrac{\Delta A_{f}}{A_{f}}f_{\alpha-}(\theta)\big]+\delta\big[f_{\delta-}(\theta)\pm\dfrac{\Delta A_{f}}{A_{f}}f_{\delta+}(\theta)\big]

(52)

\displaystyle\qquad\mp\beta\big[f_{\beta-}(\theta)\pm\dfrac{\Delta A_{f}}{A_{f}}f_{\beta+}(\theta)\big]\mp\gamma\big[f_{\gamma+}(\theta)\pm\dfrac{\Delta A_{f}}{A_{f}}f_{\gamma-}(\theta)\big],

\displaystyle\frac{Q_{f}^{\mp\pm}}{8\sin\frac{\theta}{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mp e^{\pm i\phi}\big[\alpha\hat{f}_{\alpha}(\theta)-\delta\hat{f}_{\delta-}(\theta)+(\gamma-\beta)\dfrac{\Delta A_{f}}{A_{f}}\hat{f}_{\beta\gamma}(\theta)\mp\delta\dfrac{\Delta A_{f}}{A_{f}}\hat{f}_{\delta+}(\theta)\big].

Here the following notations for form-factor functions are introduced:

\displaystyle f_{\alpha+}(\theta)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}[f_{1}(\theta)+f_{3}(\theta)]=E_{\chi}(m+\lambda^{2}_{-}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2})+\lambda^{2}_{+}\lambda^{2}_{-}=E_{\chi}(m\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+E_{p}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2})+|\bm{k}|^{2},

\displaystyle f_{\delta+}(\theta)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}[f_{4}(\theta)+f_{1}(\theta)]=E_{\chi}(m+\lambda^{2}_{-})+\lambda^{2}_{+}\lambda^{2}_{-}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}=E_{\chi}E_{p}+|\bm{k}|^{2}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2},

\displaystyle f_{\beta+}(\theta)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}[f_{5}(\theta)+f_{2}(\theta)]=\lambda_{+}\lambda_{-}(m+E_{\chi}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+\lambda^{2}_{-})=|\bm{k}|(E_{p}+E_{\chi}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}),

\displaystyle f_{\gamma+}(\theta)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}[f_{6}(\theta)+f_{2}(\theta)]=\lambda_{+}\lambda_{-}(m+E_{\chi}+\lambda^{2}_{-}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2})=|\bm{k}|(E_{\chi}+m\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+E_{p}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}),

\displaystyle f_{\alpha-}(\theta)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}[f_{3}(\theta)-f_{1}(\theta)]=\lambda_{+}^{2}\lambda^{2}_{-}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}=|\bm{k}|^{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2},

\displaystyle f_{\delta-}(\theta)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}[f_{4}(\theta)-f_{1}(\theta)]=E_{\chi}\lambda^{2}_{-}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}=E_{\chi}(E_{p}-m)\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2},

\displaystyle f_{\beta-}(\theta)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}[f_{5}(\theta)-f_{2}(\theta)]=\lambda_{+}\lambda_{-}\lambda^{2}_{-}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}=|\bm{k}|(E_{p}-m)\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2},

\displaystyle f_{\gamma-}(\theta)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}[f_{6}(\theta)-f_{2}(\theta)]=\lambda_{+}\lambda_{-}E_{\chi}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}=|\bm{k}|E_{\chi}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2};

\displaystyle\hat{f}_{\alpha}(\theta)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle f_{13}(\theta)=m_{\chi}m+m_{\chi}\lambda^{2}_{-}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}=m_{\chi}(m+(E_{p}-m)\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}),

(53)

\displaystyle\hat{f}_{\beta\gamma}(\theta)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle f_{14}(\theta)=m_{\chi}\lambda_{+}\lambda_{-}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}=m_{\chi}|\bm{k}|\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2},

\displaystyle\hat{f}_{\delta-}(\theta)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}[f_{13}(\theta)-f_{15}(\theta)]=m_{\chi}\lambda^{2}_{-}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}=m_{\chi}(E_{p}-m)\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2},

\displaystyle\hat{f}_{\delta+}(\theta)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}[f_{15}(\theta)+f_{13}(\theta)]=m_{\chi}m.

Substitution of
Q_{f}^{s^{\prime}s}
parameters from (
52
) into formula
(
40
) gives the following
expressions for
relativistic
cross sections of
coherent

\chi A
scattering of the massive
\chi

\chi A\to\chi A
)
due to weak interaction (

):

\displaystyle\frac{d\sigma^{\mp\mp}_{\text{coh}}(\bm{q})}{g_{\text{c}}\hat{c}_{A}dT_{A}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\Big|\sum_{f=p,n}G^{f}_{\alpha}(\bm{q})f_{\alpha+}(\theta)\pm\Delta G^{f}_{\alpha}(\bm{q})f_{\alpha-}(\theta)+G^{f}_{\delta}(\bm{q})f_{\delta-}(\theta)\pm\Delta G^{f}_{\delta}(\bm{q})f_{\delta+}(\theta)

\displaystyle\qquad\mp G^{f}_{\beta}(\bm{q})f_{\beta-}(\theta)-\Delta G^{f}_{\beta}(\bm{q})f_{\beta+}(\theta)\mp G^{f}_{\gamma}(\bm{q})f_{\gamma+}(\theta)-\Delta G^{f}_{\gamma}(\bm{q})f_{\gamma-}(\theta)\Big|^{2},

\displaystyle\frac{d\sigma^{\mp\pm}_{\text{coh}}(\bm{q})}{g_{\text{c}}\hat{c}_{A}dT_{A}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\Big|\sum_{f=p,n}G^{f}_{\alpha}(\bm{q})\hat{f}_{\alpha}(\theta)-G^{f}_{\delta}(\bm{q})\hat{f}_{\delta-}(\theta)\mp\Delta G^{f}_{\delta}(\bm{q})\hat{f}_{\delta+}(\theta)

(54)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+[\Delta G^{f}_{\gamma}(\bm{q})-\Delta G^{f}_{\beta}(\bm{q})]\hat{f}_{\beta\gamma}(\theta)\Big|^{2}.

In formulas (
4.3
),
coherent structural factors that accumulate dependence on both the nucleus and the weak interaction constants
are introduced:

\displaystyle G^{f}_{\alpha}(\bm{q})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\alpha_{f}F_{f}(\bm{q}){A_{f}},\qquad\Delta G^{f}_{\alpha}(\bm{q})\equiv\alpha_{f}F_{f}(\bm{q})\Delta A_{f},

\displaystyle G^{f}_{\beta}(\bm{q})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\beta_{f}F_{f}(\bm{q}){A_{f}},\qquad\Delta G^{f}_{\beta}(\bm{q})\equiv\beta_{f}F_{f}(\bm{q})\Delta A_{f},

\displaystyle G^{f}_{\gamma}(\bm{q})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\gamma_{f}F_{f}(\bm{q}){A_{f}},\qquad\Delta G^{f}_{\gamma}(\bm{q})\equiv\gamma_{f}F_{f}(\bm{q})\Delta A_{f},

(55)

\displaystyle G^{f}_{\delta}(\bm{q})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\delta_{f}F_{f}(\bm{q}){A_{f}},\qquad\Delta G^{f}_{\delta}(\bm{q})\equiv\delta_{f}F_{f}(\bm{q})\Delta A_{f}.

Important simplification can be obtained if one takes into account that masses of the proton and the neutron are the same
5
5

5

Previously it was not considered to be so, although the
f
-index of the mass
m
and the
f
-functions was omitted.
,
m\equiv m_{p}=m_{n}
.
Then the form-factor
f
-functions in (
4.3
)
will also be independent of the nucleon type, and formulas (
4.3
)
can be written as follows:

\displaystyle\frac{d\sigma^{\mp\mp}_{\text{coh}}(\bm{q})}{g_{\text{c}}\hat{c}_{A}dT_{A}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\Big|G_{\alpha}(A,\bm{q})f_{\alpha+}(\theta)\pm\Delta G_{\alpha}(A,\bm{q})f_{\alpha-}(\theta)+G_{\delta}(A,\bm{q})f_{\delta-}(\theta)\pm\Delta G_{\delta}(A,\bm{q})f_{\delta+}(\theta)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\mp G_{\beta}(A,\bm{q})f_{\beta-}(\theta)-\Delta G_{\beta}(A,\bm{q})f_{\beta+}(\theta)\mp G_{\gamma}(A,\bm{q})f_{\gamma+}(\theta)-\Delta G_{\gamma}(A,\bm{q})f_{\gamma-}(\theta)\Big|^{2},

\displaystyle\frac{d\sigma^{\mp\pm}_{\text{coh}}(\bm{q})}{g_{\text{c}}\hat{c}_{A}dT_{A}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\Big|G_{\alpha}(A,\bm{q})\hat{f}_{\alpha}(\theta)-G_{\delta}(A,\bm{q})\hat{f}_{\delta-}(\theta)\mp\Delta G_{\delta}(A,\bm{q})\hat{f}_{\delta+}(\theta)

(56)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt+[\Delta G_{\gamma}(A,\bm{q})-\Delta G_{\beta}(A,\bm{q})]\hat{f}_{\beta\gamma}(\theta)\Big|^{2}.

In (
4.3
),
new
\bm{q}
-dependent effective coupling constants are introduced,
which take into account the influence of the nuclear and nucleon structure
(
\tau
”runs through”
\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta
):

\displaystyle G_{\tau}(A,\bm{q})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle G^{p}_{\tau}(\bm{q})+G^{n}_{\tau}(\bm{q})=\tau_{p}A_{p}F_{p}(\bm{q})+\tau_{n}A_{n}F_{n}(\bm{q}),

\displaystyle\Delta G_{\tau}(A,\bm{q})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\Delta G^{p}_{\tau}(\bm{q})+\Delta G^{n}_{\tau}(\bm{q})=\tau_{p}\Delta A_{p}F_{p}(\bm{q})+\tau_{n}\Delta A_{n}F_{n}(\bm{q}).

(57)

One can say that the nuclear form factors ”participate” in the weak interaction together
with the corresponding number of (unpaired) protons or neutrons.

Taking into account expressions for the form-factor
f
-functions,
(
4.3
) can be written as

\displaystyle\frac{d\sigma^{\mp\mp}_{\text{coh}}}{g_{\text{c}}dT_{A}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\dfrac{G^{2}_{F}m_{A}}{4\pi}\dfrac{E^{2}_{\chi}}{|\bm{k}_{\chi}|^{2}}\Big|G_{\alpha}(A,\bm{q})\Big(\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+\frac{E_{p}}{m}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+\frac{|\bm{k}|^{2}}{mE_{\chi}}\Big)\pm\Delta G_{\alpha}(A,\bm{q})\frac{|\bm{k}|^{2}}{mE_{\chi}}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}

(58)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 18.49988pt+G_{\delta}(A,\bm{q})\Big(\frac{E_{p}}{m}-1\Big)\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\pm\Delta G_{\delta}(A,\bm{q})\Big(\frac{E_{p}}{m}+\frac{|\bm{k}|^{2}}{mE_{\chi}}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 9.24994pt\mp G_{\beta}(A,\bm{q})\frac{|\bm{k}|}{E_{\chi}}\Big(\frac{E_{p}}{m}-1\Big)\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}-\Delta G_{\beta}(A,\bm{q})\frac{|\bm{k}|}{m}\Big(\frac{E_{p}}{E_{\chi}}+\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 9.24994pt\mp G_{\gamma}(A,\bm{q})\frac{|\bm{k}|}{m}{\big(1+\frac{E_{p}}{E_{\chi}}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+\frac{m}{E_{\chi}}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\big)}-\Delta G_{\gamma}(A,\bm{q})\frac{|\bm{k}|}{m}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\Big|^{2},

\displaystyle\frac{d\sigma^{\mp\pm}_{\text{coh}}}{g_{\text{c}}dT_{A}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\dfrac{G^{2}_{F}m_{A}}{4\pi}\dfrac{m^{2}_{\chi}}{|\bm{k}_{\chi}|^{2}}\Big|\big(G_{\alpha}(A,\bm{q})-G_{\delta}(A,\bm{q})\big)\frac{E_{p}}{m}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+G_{\alpha}(A,\bm{q})\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+G_{\delta}(A,\bm{q})\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\big(\Delta G_{\gamma}(A,\bm{q})-\Delta G_{\beta}(A,\bm{q})\big)\frac{|\bm{k}|}{m}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\mp\Delta G_{\delta}(A,\bm{q})\Big|^{2}.

Formulas (
58
)
give the most general set of expressions for relativistic cross sections of coherent scattering of the massive
\chi
lepton
on the nucleus due to the weak interaction given the form
(
49
).
Averaged over the initial projections of
\chi
-lepton spins and summed over the final ones, the coherent cross sections are as follows:

\displaystyle\dfrac{d\sigma^{s^{\prime}=s}_{\text{coh}}}{dT_{A}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\dfrac{G^{2}_{F}m_{A}}{4\pi}\dfrac{E^{2}_{\chi}}{|\bm{k}_{\chi}|^{2}}\Big\{\Big[G_{\alpha}(A,\bm{q})\Big(\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+\frac{E_{p}}{m}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+\frac{|\bm{k}|^{2}}{mE_{\chi}}\Big)

(59)

\displaystyle+G_{\delta}(A,\bm{q})\Big(\frac{E_{p}}{m}-1\Big)\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}-\Delta G_{\beta}(A,\bm{q})\frac{|\bm{k}|}{m}\Big(\frac{E_{p}}{E_{\chi}}+\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\Big)-\Delta G_{\gamma}(A,\bm{q})\frac{|\bm{k}|}{m}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\Big]^{2}

\displaystyle+\Big[\Delta G_{\alpha}(A,\bm{q})\frac{|\bm{k}|^{2}}{mE_{\chi}}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+\Delta G_{\delta}(A,\bm{q})\Big(\frac{E_{p}}{m}+\frac{|\bm{k}|^{2}}{mE_{\chi}}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt-G_{\beta}(A,\bm{q})\frac{|\bm{k}|}{m}\frac{E_{p}-m}{E_{\chi}}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}-G_{\gamma}(A,\bm{q})\frac{|\bm{k}|}{m}{\big(1+\frac{E_{p}}{E_{\chi}}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+\frac{m}{E_{\chi}}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\big)}\Big]^{2}\Big\};

\displaystyle\dfrac{d\sigma^{s^{\prime}\neq s}_{\text{coh}}}{dT_{A}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\dfrac{G^{2}_{F}m_{A}}{4\pi}\dfrac{m^{2}_{\chi}}{|\bm{k}_{\chi}|^{2}}\Big\{\Big[G_{\alpha}(A,\bm{q})\Big(\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+\frac{E_{p}}{m}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\Big)-G_{\delta}(A,\bm{q})\Big(\frac{E_{p}}{m}-1\Big)\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt+\big(\Delta G_{\gamma}(A,\bm{q})-\Delta G_{\beta}(A,\bm{q})\big)\frac{|\bm{k}|}{m}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\Big]^{2}+\big[\Delta G_{\delta}(A,\bm{q})\big]^{2}\Big\}.

The second formula in
(
59
)
corresponds to the massive
\chi
lepton coherent scattering on the nucleus with lepton helicity (spin) flip.
The cross section in this case is proportional to the square of the lepton mass
m_{\chi}

and is strongly suppressed when the lepton escapes into the forward hemisphere.
The cross section without changing the lepton helicity (the first formula) is proportional to the square of the lepton
energy
E_{\chi}
and is maximal at the minimum lepton escape angles.
For spinless nuclei one can obtain ”shorter” formulas from
(
59
)
by simply discarding terms proportional to
\Delta G
, since for
\Delta A_{f}=0
all
\Delta G=0
.

The ”fully averaged” (averaged over the initial helicities and summed over the final lepton helicities)

coherent

\chi A
cross section is the sum of two expressions from
(
59
).
For spinless nuclei (
\Delta A_{f}=0
), this coherent
\chi A
cross section has the form

\displaystyle\dfrac{d\sigma^{\text{total}}_{\text{coh},w}}{g_{\text{c}}\hat{c}_{A}dT_{A}}\!\!

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\Big\{E^{2}_{\chi}\Big[G_{\alpha}(A,\bm{q})\big(m\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+E_{p}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+\frac{|\bm{k}|^{2}}{E_{\chi}}\big)+G_{\delta}(A,\bm{q})(E_{p}-m)\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\Big]^{2}

(60)

\displaystyle+|\bm{k}|^{2}\Big[G_{\gamma}(A,\bm{q}){[m\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+E_{p}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+E_{\chi}]}+G_{\beta}(A,\bm{q})(E_{p}-m)\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\Big]^{2}\Big\}

\displaystyle+\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}m^{2}_{\chi}\Big[G_{\alpha}(A,\bm{q})(m\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+E_{p}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2})-G_{\delta}(A,\bm{q})(E_{p}-m)\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\Big]^{2}.

As a ”simplifying example” of general formulas
(
4.3
),
the nuclear form factors are assumed to be real functions independent of the type of the nucleon, i.e.,

F(\bm{q})\equiv F_{p}(\bm{q})=F_{n}(\bm{q}).

(61)

\displaystyle\frac{d\sigma^{\mp\mp}_{\text{coh}}}{g_{\text{c}}F^{2}(\bm{q})dT_{A}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\dfrac{G^{2}_{F}m_{A}}{4\pi}\dfrac{E^{2}_{\chi}}{|\bm{k}_{\chi}|^{2}}\Big|G_{\alpha}(A)\Big(\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+\frac{E_{p}}{m}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+\frac{|\bm{k}|^{2}}{mE_{\chi}}\Big)\pm\Delta G_{\alpha}(A)\frac{|\bm{k}|^{2}}{mE_{\chi}}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}

(62)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+G_{\delta}(A)\Big(\frac{E_{p}}{m}-1\Big)\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\pm\Delta G_{\delta}(A)\Big(\frac{E_{p}}{m}+\frac{|\bm{k}|^{2}}{mE_{\chi}}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\mp G_{\beta}(A)\frac{|\bm{k}|}{E_{\chi}}\Big(\frac{E_{p}}{m}-1\Big)\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}-\Delta G_{\beta}(A)\frac{|\bm{k}|}{m}\Big(\frac{E_{p}}{E_{\chi}}+\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\mp G_{\gamma}(A)\frac{|\bm{k}|}{m}{\big(1+\frac{E_{p}}{E_{\chi}}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+\frac{m}{E_{\chi}}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\big)}-\Delta G_{\gamma}(A)\frac{|\bm{k}|}{m}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\Big|^{2},

\displaystyle\frac{d\sigma^{\mp\pm}_{\text{coh}}}{g_{\text{c}}F^{2}(\bm{q})dT_{A}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\dfrac{G^{2}_{F}m_{A}}{4\pi}\dfrac{m^{2}_{\chi}}{|\bm{k}_{\chi}|^{2}}\Big|\big(G_{\alpha}(A)-G_{\delta}(A)\big)\frac{E_{p}}{m}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+G_{\alpha}(A)\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+G_{\delta}(A)\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\big(\Delta G_{\gamma}(A)-\Delta G_{\beta}(A)\big)\frac{|\bm{k}|}{m}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\mp\Delta G_{\delta}(A)\Big|^{2}.
