current, is equal to zero.

\bm{V\!\mp\!A}
-interaction with a spin flip of the massive lepton.

In this case, general expansions of incoherent
\chi A
cross sections
(
65
)
go into the following expressions:

\displaystyle\frac{d\sigma^{\mp\pm}_{\text{inc,V-A}}}{g_{\text{i}}\hat{c}_{A}dT_{A}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{f=p,n}A_{f}\widehat{F}_{f}^{2}\Big[g_{V}^{2}\widehat{V}^{+}(\theta)+g_{A}^{2}\widehat{A}^{+}_{\mp}(\theta)+g_{V}g_{A}\widehat{M}^{+}_{\pm}(\theta)

(114)

\displaystyle\qquad+\dfrac{\Delta A_{f}}{A_{f}}\big\{g_{V}g_{A}\widehat{M}^{-}_{\pm}(\theta)-g_{V}^{2}\widehat{V}^{-}(\theta)-g_{A}^{2}\widehat{A}^{-}_{\mp}(\theta)\big\}\Big],

\displaystyle\frac{d\sigma^{\mp\pm}_{\text{inc,V+A}}}{g_{\text{i}}\hat{c}_{A}dT_{A}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{f=p,n}A_{f}\widehat{F}_{f}^{2}\Big[g_{V}^{2}\widehat{V}^{+}(\theta)+g_{A}^{2}\widehat{A}^{+}_{\pm}(\theta)-g_{V}g_{A}\widehat{M}^{+}_{\mp}(\theta)

\displaystyle\qquad+\dfrac{\Delta A_{f}}{A_{f}}\big\{g_{V}g_{A}\widehat{M}^{-}_{\mp}(\theta)+g_{V}^{2}\widehat{V}^{-}(\theta)+g_{A}^{2}\widehat{A}^{-}_{\pm}(\theta)\big\}\Big].

Here, all form-factor combinations below, which define defining incoherent
\chi A
cross sections in the case of

V\!\mp\!A
interaction
with the change of the projection of the massive lepton spin
,
depend on
G
-form-factors from (
4.3
) and
are proportional to the common factor
2m_{\chi}^{2}
:

\displaystyle\frac{\widehat{V}^{-}(\theta)}{2m_{\chi}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{G^{-}_{\alpha\gamma}(\theta)}{2m_{\chi}^{2}}=2|\bm{k}|[m+2(E_{p}-m)\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}]\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2},

\displaystyle\frac{\widehat{V}^{+}(\theta)}{2m^{2}_{\chi}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{G^{+}_{\alpha^{2}}(\theta)}{2m^{2}_{\chi}}+\frac{G^{+}_{\beta\gamma}(\theta)}{2m^{2}_{\chi}}=m^{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+|\bm{k}|^{2}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}=E^{2}_{p}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+m^{2}(\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}-\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}),

\displaystyle\frac{\widehat{A}^{-}_{\pm}(\theta)}{2m^{2}_{\chi}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{G^{-}_{\beta\delta}(\theta)}{2m^{2}_{\chi}}\pm\frac{G^{-}_{\delta^{2}}(\theta)}{2m^{2}_{\chi}}=2\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\{|\bm{k}|[m+2(E_{p}-m)\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}]\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\mp m^{2}\},

\displaystyle\frac{\widehat{M}^{-}_{\pm}(\theta)}{2m^{2}_{\chi}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{G^{-}_{\alpha\gamma}(\theta)}{2m^{2}_{\chi}}+\frac{G^{-}_{\beta\delta}(\theta)}{2m^{2}_{\chi}}\pm\frac{G^{-}_{\alpha\delta}(\theta)}{2m^{2}_{\chi}}=2|\bm{k}|\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\{m+4(E_{p}-m)\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\}\mp 2m^{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2},

\displaystyle\frac{\widehat{A}^{+}_{\pm}(\theta)}{2m^{2}_{\chi}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{G^{+}_{\delta^{2}}(\theta)}{2m^{2}_{\chi}}+\frac{G^{+}_{\beta\gamma}(\theta)}{2m^{2}_{\chi}}\pm\frac{G^{+}_{\beta\delta}(\theta)}{2m^{2}_{\chi}}=m^{2}+(m-|\bm{k}|)^{2}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\mp 4m|\bm{k}|\cos^{4}\frac{\theta}{2},

\displaystyle\frac{\widehat{M}^{+}_{\pm}(\theta)}{2m^{2}_{\chi}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{G^{+}_{\alpha\delta}(\theta)}{2m^{2}_{\chi}}-2\frac{G^{+}_{\beta\gamma}(\theta)}{2m^{2}_{\chi}}\pm\frac{G^{+}_{\beta\delta}(\theta)}{2m^{2}_{\chi}}=-2|\bm{k}|\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}[|\bm{k}|\mp m(\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}-\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2})].

Given (
38
),
incoherent
\chi A
cross sections of scattering due to
V\!\mp\!A
currents with massive lepton spin flip
can be written with dimensionless factors of the chiral constants
g_{A}
,
g_{V}
in the following final form:

\displaystyle\frac{d\sigma^{\mp\pm}_{\text{inc,V-A}}}{dT_{A}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{G^{2}_{F}m_{A}}{4\pi}\dfrac{m_{\chi}^{2}}{|\bm{k}_{\chi}|^{2}}\sum A_{f}\widehat{F}_{f}^{2}\Big[(g_{V}^{2}+g_{A}^{2})+(g_{A}^{2}-g_{V}^{2})\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}

(115)

\displaystyle+\epsilon^{2}(g_{V}-g_{A})^{2}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}-2g_{A}\epsilon[(g_{A}\mp g_{V})\mp 2(g_{A}-g_{V})\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}]\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\dfrac{2\Delta A_{f}}{A_{f}}\Big\{\epsilon(g_{A}-g_{V})[g_{A}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}(1+2\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2})+g_{V}(1+\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}-2\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2})]\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}

\displaystyle\mp g_{A}(g_{V}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+g_{A}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2})-2\epsilon\sqrt{1+\epsilon^{2}}(g_{V}-g_{A})^{2}\cos^{4}\frac{\theta}{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\Big\}\Big],

\displaystyle.\frac{d\sigma^{\mp\pm}_{\text{inc,V+A}}}{dT_{A}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{G^{2}_{F}m_{A}}{4\pi}\dfrac{m_{\chi}^{2}}{|\bm{k}_{\chi}|^{2}}\sum A_{f}\widehat{F}_{f}^{2}\Big[(g_{V}^{2}+g_{A}^{2})+(g_{A}^{2}-g_{V}^{2})\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}

\displaystyle+\epsilon^{2}(g_{V}+g_{A})^{2}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}-2g_{A}\epsilon[(g_{A}\mp g_{V})\pm 2(g_{A}+g_{V})\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}]\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\dfrac{2\Delta A_{f}}{A_{f}}\Big\{\epsilon(g_{A}+g_{V})[g_{A}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}(1-2\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2})+g_{V}(1-\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}-2\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2})]\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}

\displaystyle\pm g_{A}[g_{V}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}-g_{A}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}]+2\epsilon\sqrt{1+\epsilon^{2}}(g_{V}+g_{A})^{2}\cos^{4}\frac{\theta}{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\Big\}\Big].

Here the auxiliary notation
\dfrac{|\bm{k}|E_{p}}{m^{2}}=\epsilon\sqrt{1+\epsilon^{2}}
is introduced,
where
\epsilon\equiv\dfrac{|\bm{k}|}{m}
.
It is immediately clear that for
m_{\chi}\to 0
both formulas
(
115
)
vanish, as expected.

The limit
\epsilon\to 0
corresponds to the case where the momentum of the incident

\chi
lepton
|\bm{k}_{\chi}|\ll m
, including
|\bm{k}_{\chi}|\leq m_{\chi}\ll m
.
Then in this limit the following approximation is obtained
from formulas
(
115
):

\displaystyle\frac{d\sigma^{\mp\pm}_{\text{inc,V-A}}}{dT_{A}}=\dfrac{G^{2}_{F}m_{A}}{4\pi}\dfrac{m_{\chi}^{2}}{|\bm{k}_{\chi}|^{2}}\sum A_{f}\widehat{F}_{f}^{2}\Big[(g_{V}^{2}+g_{A}^{2})+(g_{A}^{2}-g_{V}^{2})\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\mp\dfrac{2\Delta A_{f}}{A_{f}}g_{A}\Big\{g_{V}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+g_{A}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\Big\}\Big],

\displaystyle\frac{d\sigma^{\mp\pm}_{\text{inc,V+A}}}{dT_{A}}=\dfrac{G^{2}_{F}m_{A}}{4\pi}\dfrac{m_{\chi}^{2}}{|\bm{k}_{\chi}|^{2}}\sum A_{f}\widehat{F}_{f}^{2}\Big[(g_{V}^{2}+g_{A}^{2})+(g_{A}^{2}-g_{V}^{2})\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\pm\dfrac{2\Delta A_{f}}{A_{f}}g_{A}\Big\{g_{V}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}-g_{A}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\Big\}\Big].

It shows that for the nucleus with spin 0 (
\Delta A_{f}=0
) when
\epsilon\simeq 0
these cross sections coincide

\frac{d\sigma^{\mp\pm}_{\text{inc,V-A}}}{dT_{A}}=\frac{d\sigma^{\mp\pm}_{\text{inc,V+A}}}{dT_{A}}=\dfrac{G^{2}_{F}m_{A}}{4\pi}\dfrac{m_{\chi}^{2}}{|\bm{k}_{\chi}|^{2}}\sum A_{f}\widehat{F}_{f}^{2}(\bm{q})\Big[(g_{V}^{2}+g_{A}^{2})+(g_{A}^{2}-g_{V}^{2})\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\Big].

However, this observation is of no practical use, since at such a small momentum of the incident lepton
the value of the transferred momentum is
\bm{q}\simeq 0
, which results in
\widehat{F}_{f}^{2}(\bm{q})\simeq 0
.
In other words, for sufficiently small transferred momenta, one can not speak about any
noticeable role of inelastic (incoherent) scattering processes.

Fully averaged incoherent ”
\bm{V\!\mp\!A}
” cross section.

Recall that the fully averaged (averaged over the initial and summed over the final helicities of the
\chi
particle)

incoherent

\chi A
-interaction cross section is given by
(
75
).
In the
V\mp A
approximation, when

\alpha_{\mp}=+g_{V},\ \beta_{\mp}=-g_{A},\ \gamma_{\mp}=\mp g_{V},\ \delta_{\mp}=\pm g_{A}

one has

\displaystyle\alpha^{2}_{\mp}=\gamma^{2}_{\mp}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g_{V}^{2},\quad\beta^{2}_{\mp}=\delta^{2}_{\mp}=g_{A}^{2},\quad(\gamma^{2}-\beta^{2})_{\mp}=(g_{V}^{2}-g_{A}^{2}),

\displaystyle(\alpha-\delta)_{\mp}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(g_{V}\mp g_{A}),\quad(\beta-\gamma)_{\mp}=\pm(g_{V}\mp g_{A}),

\displaystyle(\alpha-\delta)\delta_{\mp}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pm(g_{V}\mp g_{A})g_{A},\quad(\beta-\gamma)\beta_{\mp}=\mp(g_{V}\mp g_{A})g_{A},

(116)

\displaystyle(\alpha\gamma+\beta\delta)_{\mp}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mp(g_{V}^{2}+g_{A}^{2}),\quad(\alpha\delta+\beta\gamma)_{\mp}=\pm 2g_{V}g_{A},\quad(\alpha\beta+\gamma\delta)_{\mp}=-2g_{V}g_{A}.

Then sums (
81
)
defining completely averaged incoherent
\chi A
cross sections
go into the following ”
V\pm A
” expressions:

\displaystyle\sum_{s^{\prime},s}\frac{Q^{s^{\prime}s}_{+,\text{V$\mp$A}}}{2^{6}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(g_{V}^{2}+3g_{A}^{2})m^{2}m^{2}_{\chi}\pm 4g_{V}g_{A}|\bm{k}|^{2}s+4g_{A}(g_{A}\mp g_{V})m^{2}|\bm{k}|^{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}

(117)

\displaystyle+(g_{V}\mp g_{A})^{2}|\bm{k}|^{2}[4|\bm{k}|^{2}\sin^{4}\frac{\theta}{2}+2s\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+m^{2}_{\chi}(\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+\sin^{4}\frac{\theta}{2}+\cos^{4}\frac{\theta}{2})],

\displaystyle\sum_{s^{\prime},s}\frac{Q^{s^{\prime}s}_{-,\text{V$\mp$A}}}{2^{7}|\bm{k}|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mp(g_{V}\mp g_{A})^{2}[m^{2}_{\chi}E_{p}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}(1-2\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2})-2|\bm{k}|^{2}(E_{p}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+E_{\chi})\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}]

\displaystyle\mp(g_{V}^{2}-g_{A}^{2})m^{2}_{\chi}m\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}(1-2\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2})+g_{A}^{2}[mm^{2}_{\chi}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}(1-2\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2})\pm 2m^{2}E_{\chi}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}]

\displaystyle+g_{V}g_{A}[mm^{2}_{\chi}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}(1-2\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2})+2m^{2}_{\chi}E_{p}+4|\bm{k}|^{2}(E_{\chi}+E_{p})+2m^{2}E_{\chi}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}].

As a result, the fully averaged
incoherent

\chi A
interaction cross section
(
75
)
with allowance for sums from
(
117
)
takes the ”
V\!\mp\!A
” form

\displaystyle\frac{d\sigma^{\text{total}}_{\text{inc,V$\mp$A}}}{g_{\text{i}}dT_{A}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{G^{2}_{F}m_{A}}{4\pi}\dfrac{1}{m^{2}|\bm{k}_{\chi}|^{2}}\sum_{f=p,n}A_{f}\widehat{F}^{2}_{f}(\bm{q})\Big[\sum_{s^{\prime}s}\frac{Q^{s^{\prime}s}_{+,\text{V$\mp$A}}}{2^{6}}+\dfrac{2\Delta A_{f}}{A_{f}}|\bm{k}|\sum_{s^{\prime}s}\frac{Q^{s^{\prime}s}_{-,\text{V$\mp$A}}}{2^{7}|\bm{k}|}\Big].

Further, since the
V\!\mp\!A
limit satisfies the relations
(
4.4
),
there are the following expressions
then for parameters from (
83
):

\displaystyle\Phi^{+}_{\alpha^{2}+3\delta^{2}}(\bm{q})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Phi^{+}_{g_{V}^{2}}+3\Phi^{+}_{g_{A}^{2}}(\bm{q}),\hskip 9.24994pt\Phi^{+}_{\gamma^{2}-\beta^{2}}(\bm{q})=\Phi^{+}_{g_{V}^{2}}(\bm{q})-\Phi^{+}_{g_{A}^{2}}(\bm{q}),

\displaystyle\Phi^{+}_{(\beta-\gamma)^{2}}(\bm{q})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Phi^{+}_{(g_{V}\mp g_{A})^{2}}(\bm{q}),\hskip 18.49988pt\Phi^{+}_{(\alpha-\delta)^{2}+(\beta-\gamma)^{2}}(\bm{q})=2\Phi^{+}_{(g_{V}\mp g_{A})^{2}}(\bm{q}),

\displaystyle\Phi^{+}_{2(\alpha\delta+\beta\gamma)}(\bm{q})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pm 4\Phi^{+}_{g_{V}g_{A}}(\bm{q}),\hskip 9.24994pt\Phi^{+}_{(\beta-\gamma)\beta}(\bm{q})=\Phi^{+}_{(\delta-\alpha)\delta}(\bm{q})=\mp\Phi^{+}_{g_{V}g_{A}\mp g_{A}^{2}}(\bm{q}).

After their substituting into general formula
(
83
),
for the spinless nucleus there is

\displaystyle\frac{d\sigma^{\text{total}}_{\text{inc,V$\mp$A},0}}{g_{\text{i}}\hat{c}_{A}dT_{A}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[\Phi^{+}_{g_{V}^{2}}(\bm{q})+3\Phi^{+}_{g_{A}^{2}}(\bm{q})]m^{2}_{\chi}m^{2}\pm 4\Phi^{+}_{g_{V}g_{A}}(\bm{q})|\bm{k}|^{2}s+4\Phi^{+}_{(g_{A}\mp g_{V})g_{A}}(\bm{q})m^{2}|\bm{k}|^{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}

\displaystyle.+\Phi^{+}_{(g_{V}\mp g_{A})^{2}}(\bm{q})|\bm{k}|^{2}[4|\bm{k}|^{2}\sin^{4}\frac{\theta}{2}+2s\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+m^{2}_{\chi}(\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+2\sin^{4}\frac{\theta}{2})].

Here, by analogy with the definitions from (
69
), the following notations are given:

\displaystyle\Phi^{+}_{V^{2}}(\bm{q})\equiv\Phi^{+}_{g_{V}^{2}}(\bm{q})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{f=p,n}\widehat{F}_{f}^{2}(\bm{q})A_{f}[g_{V}^{f}]^{2},\quad\Phi^{+}_{VA}(\bm{q})\equiv\Phi^{+}_{g_{V}g_{A}}(\bm{q})=\sum_{f=p,n}\widehat{F}_{f}^{2}(\bm{q})A_{f}g^{f}_{A}g^{f}_{V},

\displaystyle\Phi^{+}_{A^{2}}(\bm{q})\equiv\Phi^{+}_{g_{A}^{2}}(\bm{q})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{f=p,n}\widehat{F}_{f}^{2}(\bm{q})A_{f}[g_{A}^{f}]^{2}.

(118)

With these notations, the fully averaged incoherent
\chi A
cross section on the
spinless
nucleus
in the form of an expansion in chiral coupling constants
g_{V}
and
g_{A}
takes the form

\displaystyle\frac{d\sigma^{\text{total}}_{\text{inc,V$\mp$A},0}}{g_{\text{i}}dT_{A}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\dfrac{G^{2}_{F}m_{A}}{4\pi m^{2}|\bm{k}_{\chi}|^{2}}\Big\{\Phi^{+}_{V^{2}}(\bm{q})S_{V^{2}}(\theta)+\Phi^{+}_{A^{2}}(\bm{q})S_{A^{2}}(\theta)\pm 2\Phi^{+}_{VA}(\bm{q})S_{VA}(\theta)\Big\},\hskip 9.24994pt\text{where}

(119)

\displaystyle S_{V^{2}}(\theta)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle m^{2}_{\chi}m^{2}+4|\bm{k}|^{4}\sin^{4}\frac{\theta}{2}+2s|\bm{k}|^{2}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+m^{2}_{\chi}|\bm{k}|^{2}(\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+2\sin^{4}\frac{\theta}{2}),

\displaystyle S_{A^{2}}(\theta)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle 3m^{2}_{\chi}m^{2}+4m^{2}|\bm{k}|^{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+|\bm{k}|^{2}[4|\bm{k}|^{2}\sin^{4}\frac{\theta}{2}+2s\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+m^{2}_{\chi}(\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+2\sin^{4}\frac{\theta}{2})],

\displaystyle S_{VA}(\theta)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle|\bm{k}|^{2}\big[2s-2m^{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}-[4|\bm{k}|^{2}\sin^{4}\frac{\theta}{2}+2s\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+m^{2}_{\chi}(\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+2\sin^{4}\frac{\theta}{2})]\big].

For the
massless

\chi
lepton (
m_{\chi}=0
), summation over all lepton helicities
(
117
)
gives the following result:

\displaystyle\sum\frac{Q^{s^{\prime}s}_{+,\text{V$\mp$A},0}}{2^{7}|\bm{k}|^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+2g_{A}^{2}m^{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\pm 2g_{V}g_{A}(s-m^{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2})+(g_{V}\mp g_{A})^{2}(2|\bm{k}|^{2}\sin^{4}\frac{\theta}{2}+s\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}),

\displaystyle\sum\frac{Q^{s^{\prime}s}_{-,\text{V$\mp$A},0}}{2^{7}|\bm{k}|^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pm 2g_{A}^{2}m^{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+2g_{V}g_{A}(s-m^{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2})

(120)

\displaystyle\pm(g_{V}\mp g_{A})^{2}((s-m^{2})\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+2|\bm{k}|^{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2})\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}.

Then, for
m_{\chi}=0
in the
V\!\mp\!A
variant, the fully averaged
incoherent
\chi A
cross section is

\displaystyle\frac{d\sigma^{\text{total}}_{\text{inc,V$\mp$A},0}}{g_{\text{i}}dT_{A}}\!

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\dfrac{G^{2}_{F}m_{A}}{2\pi}\sum_{f=p,n}A_{f}\widehat{F}^{2}_{f}(\bm{q})\Big\{2g_{A}^{2}\frac{m^{2}}{s}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+(g_{V}\mp g_{A})^{2}\big[\dfrac{1}{2}\big(1-\dfrac{m^{2}}{s}\big)^{2}\sin^{4}\frac{\theta}{2}+\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\big]

(121)

\displaystyle\pm 2g_{V}g_{A}\big(1-\frac{m^{2}}{s}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\big)+\dfrac{\Delta A_{f}}{A_{f}}\Big[2g_{V}g_{A}\big(1-\frac{m^{2}}{s}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\big)\pm 2g_{A}^{2}\frac{m^{2}}{s}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\pm(g_{V}\mp g_{A})^{2}\big(1-\frac{m^{2}}{s}\big)\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\big[\dfrac{1}{2}\big(1-\dfrac{m^{2}}{s}\big)\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\big]\Big]\Big\}.

This
\chi A
cross section, like each expression
(
4.4
)
corresponding to the
massless
lepton, despite the presence of a sum over
lepton helicities, generally speaking, contains only one term.
For the
V\!-\!A
variant, it is the only contribution of the lepton with the negative
conserved
helicity
(neutrino, same as formula (
113
)).
For the
V\!+\!A
variant, it is the only contribution of the lepton with positive

conserved
helicity (antineutrino).
This expression coincides with the formulas for (anti)neutrinos from
Bednyakov:2021ppn
.

Total averaged
{\chi A}
cross section for the massive lepton and
\bm{V\!\mp\!A}
currents.

It is the sum of two terms:
the fully averaged coherent (
104
)
and fully averaged incoherent (
119
)

\chi A
interaction cross sections due to
V\!\mp\!A
currents.
Let us present this expression for the spinless nucleus, taking into account (
46
)
in the form of expansion in the effective
V\!\mp\!A
constants
(assuming that
g_{\text{i}}\simeq g_{\text{c}}\simeq 1
)

\displaystyle\dfrac{d\sigma^{\text{total}}_{V\!\mp\!A,0}}{dT_{A}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{G^{2}_{F}m_{A}}{4\pi}\Big\{\Phi^{+}_{V^{2}}(\bm{q})S_{V^{2}}(\theta)+\Phi^{+}_{A^{2}}(\bm{q})S_{A^{2}}(\theta)\pm 2\Phi^{+}_{VA}(\bm{q})S_{VA}(\theta)

(122)

\displaystyle\qquad+G_{V}^{2}(\bm{q})C_{V}(\theta)+G_{A}^{2}(\bm{q})C_{A}(\theta)\pm 2G_{V}(\bm{q})G_{A}(\bm{q})C_{M}(\theta)\Big\}.

Here the coefficients in front of the effective
V\!\mp\!A
constants are redefined by including into them
the common factor
(|\bm{k}|^{2}s)^{-1}
, i.e.,

S_{V^{2}}(\theta)\equiv\dfrac{S_{V^{2}}(\theta)}{|\bm{k}|^{2}s}
, etc.
They have the following form:

\displaystyle S_{V^{2}}(\theta)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{m^{2}_{\chi}m^{2}}{|\bm{k}|^{2}s}+2\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+\dfrac{4|\bm{k}|^{2}}{s}\sin^{4}\frac{\theta}{2}+\dfrac{m^{2}_{\chi}}{s}\Big(\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+2\sin^{4}\frac{\theta}{2}\Big),

\displaystyle S_{A^{2}}(\theta)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{3m^{2}_{\chi}m^{2}}{|\bm{k}|^{2}s}+\dfrac{4m^{2}}{s}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+2\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+\dfrac{4|\bm{k}|^{2}}{s}\sin^{4}\frac{\theta}{2}+\dfrac{m^{2}_{\chi}}{s}\Big(\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+2\sin^{4}\frac{\theta}{2}\Big),

\displaystyle S_{VA}(\theta)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\Big(1-\dfrac{m^{2}}{s}\Big)-\dfrac{4|\bm{k}|^{2}}{s}\sin^{4}\frac{\theta}{2}-\dfrac{m^{2}_{\chi}}{s}\Big(\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+2\sin^{4}\frac{\theta}{2}\Big);

\displaystyle C_{A}(\theta)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{E_{p}}{\sqrt{s}}-\frac{m}{\sqrt{s}}\Big)^{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\Big(\frac{m_{\chi}^{2}}{|\bm{k}|^{2}}+2\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\Big),

\displaystyle C_{M}(\theta)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\Big(\frac{E_{p}}{\sqrt{s}}-\frac{m}{\sqrt{s}}\Big)\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\Big(\frac{E_{\chi}}{\sqrt{s}}+\frac{m}{\sqrt{s}}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+\frac{E_{p}}{\sqrt{s}}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\Big),

\displaystyle C_{V}(\theta)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{m}{\sqrt{s}}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+\frac{E_{p}}{\sqrt{s}}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\Big)^{2}\Big(\frac{m_{\chi}^{2}}{|\bm{k}|^{2}}+2\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\Big)

\displaystyle+\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\Big(\dfrac{m_{\chi}^{2}}{s}+\dfrac{2|\bm{k}|^{2}}{s}+\dfrac{4E_{\chi}}{\sqrt{s}}\Big(\dfrac{m}{\sqrt{s}}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+\dfrac{E_{p}}{\sqrt{s}}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\Big)\Big).

Formula (
122
) can also be obtained
by the corresponding substitutions into the general expression
(
86
), which
holds for the total cross section of the weak interaction of the massive lepton and the spinless nucleus.
The other variables included in expression
(
122
)
are defined in the text of this chapter by formulas
(
4.4
)
and (
4.4
).

Note
that expression (
122
)
can be considered
as a new prescription
for computing the
cross sections of the (massive) lepton scattering on the nucleus via weak
V\!\mp\!A
interaction
at energies below hundreds of MeV.
For example, such modern software products as
Achilles
from
Isaacson:2022cwh

claiming to allow precision description of weak processes including those considered in this paper
should apparently take into account the results obtained here.

5
Conclusions

This paper outlines all elements of the theoretical approach to the description of the scattering of a
massive
(neutral) lepton on a nucleus as an object, the internal structure of which is due to
mutually interacting nucleons.
This is a natural generalization of the new concept in the theory of
(anti)neutrino scattering on the nucleus as a composite system proposed by D.V. Naumov and the author
in

Bednyakov:2018mjd
;
Bednyakov:2019dbl
;
Bednyakov:2021ppn
;
Bednyakov:2021bty
.
The boundary condition for the applicability of the approach is the requirement to preserve
integrity of the nucleus after interaction.
The nucleus as a complex quantum system can be ”excited”, can pass from one of its quantum states to another,
but in the canonical version of this approach, the nucleus should not ”fall apart”.
This requirement imposes restrictions on the magnitude of the momentum transferred by the lepton to the nucleus,
which should not be so large as to completely ”destroy the nucleus”.
In addition, the applicability of the approach is, strictly speaking,
also limited by the approximations made, which, in turn, are
explicitly formulated in the text, look quite natural, and,
if necessary, can be specially investigated and taken into account as,
for example, correction factors or contributions.

The approach constructively uses the so-called condition for the completeness of the quantum state of the nucleus (or the probability conservation condition), which, being based on the summation over all possible initial and final states of the nucleus,
allows one to explicitly separate the elastic process from all other inelastic processes
capable of contributing to the total observable
\chi A
scattering cross section.
The approach further relies on the microscopic description of the nucleus as a bound state
of its constituent nucleons on the basis of the many-particle wave function of the nucleus.
The
weak
interaction of a point-like
\chi
lepton is considered to be between structureless protons and neutrons of the nucleus.
This interaction is parameterized
in the form of an expansion of the scalar product of the
lepton and nucleon currents in four effective coupling constants
reflecting the (axial) vector nature of the weak interaction

\displaystyle(l^{w}_{s^{\prime}s},h^{w,f}_{r^{\prime}r})=\alpha_{f}(l^{v}_{s^{\prime}s}\,h^{v}_{r^{\prime}r})+\beta_{f}(l^{v}_{s^{\prime}s}\,h^{a}_{r^{\prime}r})+\gamma_{f}(l^{a}_{s^{\prime}s}\,h^{v}_{r^{\prime}r})+\delta_{f}(l^{a}_{s^{\prime}s}\ ,h^{a}_{r^{\prime}r}).

Relativistic
expressions for the cross sections of the massive (neutral)
\chi
lepton scattering
on the nucleus are obtained in a general form.
It is shown that the
observable
cross section of the process
\chi A\to\chi A^{(*)}

(
86
)
includes the elastic (or coherent) contribution
(
58
) when the nucleus
remains in its original quantum state and the inelastic (incoherent) contribution
(
65
)
when the nucleus goes into another (excited) quantum state.
Transition from the elastic scattering regime to the inelastic scattering regime
is
automatically
determined by the dependence of the nucleon-nucleus form factors

F_{p/n}(\bm{q})
on the momentum
\bm{q}
transferred to the nucleus.
At small
\bm{q}
the elastic scattering dominates, and as
\bm{q}
increases
the contribution of the inelastic scattering increases,
and the latter dominates at sufficiently large
\bm{q}
.
This
automatic behavior
makes this approach fundamentally different
from Friedmann’s concept of coherence

Freedman:1973yd
;
Freedman:1977xn
,
within which, before using formulas for the coherent scattering of the (anti)neutrino on the nucleus,
one has to make sure in advance that this can be done, i.e.,
that the product of the characteristic radius of the target nucleus
and some characteristic momentum transferred to the nucleus is noticeably less than unity (
|\bm{q}|R\ll 1
).
In our approach, this question does not arise at all.

As an important application of the general formulas, the scattering of
massive
(anti)neutrinos
interacting with nucleons through the
V\!\mp\!A
current of the Standard Model is considered in detail.
The weak
V\!-\!A
interaction with the nucleus corresponds to the massive analogue of the neutrino (negative helicity),
and the case of the weak
V\!+\!A
interaction is the massive analog of the antineutrino (positive helicity).
A complete set of expressions for the corresponding cross sections is obtained
(e.g. formulas
(
4.4
)
and (
111
)).
The transition of these formulas to the formulas from
Bednyakov:2021ppn

corresponding to the scattering of
massless
(anti)neutrinos of the Standard Model
on the nucleus is demonstrated.

Owing to the nonzero mass of the (anti)neutrino, an additional channel for the elastic
(coherent) and inelastic (incoherent) scattering of (anti)neutrinos on the nuclei arises from
the possibility of a change in helicity in massive (anti)neutrinos.
For example, despite the smallness of the mass of neutrinos at kinetic energies of (anti)neutrinos much lower than the mass of neutrinos (for example, relict ones), the cross section of their interaction with the nucleus turns out to be multiply enhanced by the
”nucleus coherence effect”.

The resulting expressions can be used, for example, in the analysis of
results of experiments on the direct detection of neutral massive weakly interacting
relativistic and non-relativistic particles of dark matter,
since, unlike the generally accepted case,
both the elastic and inelastic interactions of such particles with the target nucleus
are simultaneously taken into account.
In this case, the presence of the ”inelastic signal” with its characteristic signature in the form of

\gamma
-quanta from deexcitation of the nucleus may be the only
registered evidence

Bednyakov:2021ppn
;
Bednyakov:2021bty
;
Bednyakov:2022dmc

of the interaction of the dark matter particle.

6
Appendix

Wave functions.

Recall
Bednyakov:2018mjd
;
Bednyakov:2019dbl
;
Bednyakov:2021ppn
that
the state of the nucleus is denoted by the symbol
|P_{l}\rangle
, which means that the nucleus has a 4-momentum
P_{l}
, is in some
l
th internal quantum state (
l=n,m
) and the
|P_{l}\rangle

is a superposition of free nucleons
|\{p\}\rangle
multiplied by the wave function of the bound state
\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}(\{p\})
.
The latter is a product of the wave function
\widetilde{\psi}_{n}(\{p{{}^{\star}}\})
, which describes the internal structure of the nucleus in its rest frame (the corresponding momenta are marked with index
\star
), and the wave function
\Phi_{n}(p)
responsible for the motion of the nucleus as a whole with the momentum
\bm{p}=\sum^{A}_{i=1}\bm{p}_{i}
and the projection of the nuclear spin
s

\widetilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}(\{p\})=\widetilde{\psi}_{n}(\{p{{}^{\star}}\})\Phi_{n}(p),\quad\text{where}\quad p=(\bm{p},s).

(123)

The function
\Phi_{n}(p)
depends on
A-1
terms of 3-momenta, since one combination of
A
3-momenta is used to describe the motion of the nucleus as a whole. Taking into account (
123
) for the state
|P_{n}\rangle
, which completely characterizes the nucleus
A
, we will use the (antisymmetrized) expression
(
13
) from the main text of the paper.

For nuclear states
|n\rangle
describing the nucleus at rest in the
n
th internal quantum state (
n
th level), the normalization condition is adopted in the form

\langle m|n\rangle\equiv\int\Big(\prod^{A}_{i}\frac{d\bm{p}^{\star}_{i}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\Big)\widetilde{\psi}_{n}(\{p^{\star}\})\widetilde{\psi}^{*}_{m}(\{p^{\star}\})(2\pi)^{3}\delta^{3}(\sum^{A}_{i=1}\bm{p}^{\star}_{i})=\delta_{mn}.

(124)

For nuclear states
|P_{n}\rangle
from (
13
) it gives the normalization condition

\langle P^{\prime}_{m}|P_{n}\rangle=(2\pi)^{3}2P^{0}_{n}\delta^{3}(\bm{P}-\bm{P}^{\prime})\delta_{nm}.

(125)

A Formal definition of the
|n\rangle
-state satisfying
(
124
) can be given as follows:

|n\rangle=\int\Big(\prod_{i=1}^{A}d\widetilde{\bm{p}}_{i}^{\star}\Big)\frac{\widetilde{\psi}_{n}(\{p{{}^{\star}}\})}{\sqrt{A!}}\Big[(2\pi)^{3}\delta^{3}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{A}\bm{p}_{i}^{\star}\Big)\Big]^{1/2}|\{p^{\star}\}\rangle.

(126)

Formula for the hadronic current
\bm{h^{\mu}_{mn}(q)}
.

The definition of the matrix element in terms of the effective Lagrangian

\mathcal{L}_{\rm int}(x)=\dfrac{G_{\rm F}}{\sqrt{2}}H^{\mu}(x)\,L_{\mu}(x)
looks as follows:

\displaystyle i(2\pi)^{4}\delta^{4}(\sum p_{i}-\!\sum p_{f})\mathcal{M}\equiv\langle{\rm f}|i\!\int\!d^{4}x\mathcal{L}_{\rm int}(x)|{\rm i}\rangle=\langle{\rm f}|\dfrac{iG_{\rm F}}{\sqrt{2}}\!\int\!\!d^{4}x\!:\!H^{\mu}(x)L_{\mu}(x)\!:\!|{\rm i}\rangle.

(127)

In the case of
\chi A\to\chi A^{(*)}
scattering, the initial and final states are

\displaystyle|{\rm i}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\chi(k,s),A(P_{n})\rangle=(2\pi)^{3/2}\sqrt{2E_{\chi}}\,a^{+}_{\chi}(\bm{k},s)|0\rangle|P_{n}\rangle,

\displaystyle\langle{\rm f}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle\chi(k^{\prime},s^{\prime}),A^{(*)}(P^{\prime}_{m})|=(2\pi)^{3/2}\sqrt{2E_{\chi^{\prime}}}\,\langle 0|a_{\chi}(\bm{k}^{\prime},s^{\prime})\langle P^{\prime}_{m}|.

Therefore, expression (
127
)
for the process
\chi A\to\chi A^{(*)}
becomes

\displaystyle(2\pi)^{4}\delta^{4}(k+P_{n}-k^{\prime}-P^{\prime}_{m})i\mathcal{M}_{mn}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{iG_{\rm F}}{\sqrt{2}}\int d^{4}x\,H^{\mu}_{nm}(x)\,L_{\mu}^{\chi}(x),\quad\text{where}

(129)

\displaystyle H^{\mu}_{nm}(x)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\langle P^{\prime}_{m}|:\mathrel{\mspace{1mu}\hat{H}^{\mu}(x)\mspace{1mu}}:|P_{n}\rangle,

\displaystyle L_{\mu}^{\chi}(x)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle(2\pi)^{3}\sqrt{2E_{\chi^{\prime}}2E_{\chi}}\langle 0|a_{\chi}(\bm{k}^{\prime},s^{\prime}):\mathrel{\mspace{1mu}\hat{L}_{\mu}(x)\mspace{1mu}}:a^{+}_{\chi}(\bm{k},s)|0\rangle.

Using the fermionic quantum field operators of the
\chi
particle and the nucleon

\displaystyle\overline{\hat{\psi}}(x)=\int\frac{d\bm{y}^{\prime}e^{iy^{\prime}x}}{\sqrt{(2\pi)^{3}2E_{\bm{y}^{\prime}}}}\sum_{s=1,2}a^{+}_{s}(\bm{y}^{\prime})\overline{u}(\bm{y}^{\prime},s),\ \ \hat{\psi}(x)=\int\frac{d\bm{y}e^{-iyx}}{\sqrt{(2\pi)^{3}2E_{\bm{y}}}}\sum_{r=1,2}a_{r}(\bm{y})u(\bm{y},r),\quad

(130)

one can write the nucleon
\hat{H}^{\mu}(x)
and the lepton
\hat{L}_{\mu}(x)
operators
in the following way:

\hat{H}^{\mu}(x)\equiv\sum^{A}_{k}\overline{\hat{\psi}}_{k}(x)\,O^{\mu}_{k}\,\hat{\psi}_{k}(x)\quad\text{and}\quad\hat{L}_{\mu}(x)\equiv\overline{\hat{\psi}}_{\chi}(x)\,O_{\mu}\,\hat{\psi}_{\chi}(x),

where
O^{\mu}
is some combination of
\gamma
-matrices corresponding to the
specific Lorentz interaction structure.
Then the lepton element from formula (
129
)
is transformed as follows:

\displaystyle L_{\mu}^{\chi}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{E_{k^{\prime}}E_{k}}\int\frac{d\bm{y}^{\prime}d\bm{y}e^{ix(y^{\prime}-y)}}{\sqrt{E_{\bm{y}^{\prime}}E_{\bm{y}}}}\sum_{r,r^{\prime}=1,2}\langle 0|a_{s^{\prime}}(\bm{k}^{\prime})\!:\!a^{+}_{r^{\prime}}(\bm{y}^{\prime})\overline{u}(\bm{y}^{\prime},r^{\prime})O_{\mu}a_{r}(\bm{y})u(\bm{y},r)\!:\!a^{+}_{s}(\bm{k})|0\rangle.

Further, since
a_{s^{\prime}}(\bm{k}^{\prime})a^{+}_{r^{\prime}}(\bm{y}^{\prime})=\delta_{s^{\prime},r^{\prime}}\delta(\bm{k}^{\prime}-\bm{y}^{\prime})\text{~~and~~}a_{r}(\bm{y})a^{+}_{s}(\bm{k})=\delta_{s,r}\delta(\bm{k}-\bm{y})
, one gets

L_{\mu}^{\chi}(x)=e^{ix(k^{\prime}-k)}\overline{u}_{\chi}(\bm{k}^{\prime},s^{\prime})O_{\mu}u_{\chi}(\bm{k},s)\equiv e^{-ixq}\,\overline{u}_{\chi}(\bm{k}^{\prime},s^{\prime})O_{\mu}u_{\chi}(\bm{k},s).

(131)

According to (
13
),
the initial and final wave functions of the nucleus are given by the formulas

\displaystyle(2\pi)^{3}\sqrt{2P^{0^{\prime}}_{m}}\delta^{3}(\bm{p}^{\prime}-\bm{P}^{\prime}_{m})\cdot(2\pi)^{3}\sqrt{2P^{0}_{n}}\delta^{3}(\bm{p}-\bm{P}_{n}),

Formula for the hadronic structure
{f^{k}_{mn}(\bm{q})}
.

Let us demonstrate that the multidimensional integral in formula
(

) is
the matrix element
\langle m|e^{i\bm{q}\hat{\bm{X}}_{k}}|n\rangle
.
To this end, we note that the operator
\displaystyle e^{i\bm{q}\hat{\bm{X}}}
increases the 3-momentum
\bm{p}
of the state
|\bm{p}\rangle
by momentum
\bm{q}
, transforming the state

|\bm{p}\rangle
into the state
|\bm{p}+\bm{q}\rangle
by the operation
13
13

13

Justification of formula (

Bednyakov:2021ppn
.

e^{i\bm{q}\hat{\bm{X}}}|\bm{p}\rangle=\frac{\sqrt{2E_{\bm{p}}}}{\sqrt{2E_{\bm{p}+\bm{q}}}}|\bm{p}+\bm{q}\rangle.

(142)

From (
142
) and the normalization condition of the single-particle states

\langle\bm{k}|\bm{p}\rangle=(2\pi)^{3}2E_{\bm{p}}\delta^{3}(\bm{p}-\bm{k})
one gets

\langle\bm{k}|e^{i\bm{q}\hat{\bm{X}}}|\bm{p}\rangle=\frac{\sqrt{2E_{\bm{p}}}}{\sqrt{2E_{\bm{p}+\bm{q}}}}\langle\bm{k}|\bm{p}+\bm{q}\rangle=(2\pi)^{3}\sqrt{2E_{\bm{p}}2E_{\bm{p}+\bm{q}}}\delta^{3}(\bm{p}+\bm{q}-\bm{k}).

(143)

By definition, the matrix element
\langle m|e^{i\bm{q}\hat{\bm{X}}_{k}}|n\rangle
should be calculated in the nuclear rest frame,
where the nuclear state wave function
|n\rangle
is given by (
126
).
Let us write them in the form

\displaystyle\langle m|=\!\int\!\phi^{*}_{m}(0)\Big(\prod^{A}_{j}d\tilde{\bm{p}^{\prime}}^{\star}_{j}\Big)\frac{\tilde{\psi}^{*}_{m}(\{p^{\prime}_{\star}\})}{\sqrt{{A!}}}\!\langle\{p^{\prime}_{\star}\}|\text{~~and~~}|n\rangle=\int\!\phi_{n}(0)\Big(\prod^{A}_{i}d\tilde{\bm{p}}^{\star}_{i}\Big)\frac{\tilde{\psi}_{n}(\{p_{\star}\})}{\sqrt{{A!}}}|\{p_{\star}\}\rangle.\qquad

With (
144
),

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\langle\{p^{\prime}_{\star}\}|e^{i\bm{q}\hat{\bm{X}}_{k}}|\{p_{\star}\}\rangle=\langle...,p^{{}^{\prime}\star}_{k},...|e^{i\bm{q}\hat{\bm{X}}_{k}}|...,p^{\star}_{k},...\rangle.

It can be presented as a product of

\displaystyle\langle...,p^{{}^{\prime}\star}_{k-1},p^{{}^{\prime}\star}_{k+1},...|...,p^{\star}_{k-1},p^{\star}_{k+1},...\rangle=(A-1)!\Big[\prod^{A-1}_{l\neq k}(2\pi)^{3}2E_{\bm{p}^{\star}_{l}}\delta^{3}(\bm{p}^{\star}_{l}-\bm{p}^{{}^{\prime}\star}_{l})\delta_{r_{l},r^{\prime}_{l}}\Big],

\displaystyle\langle...,p^{{}^{\prime}\star}_{k-1},p^{{}^{\prime}\star}_{k+1},...|...,p^{\star}_{k-1},p^{\star}_{k+1},...\rangle\langle\bm{p}^{{}^{\prime}\star}_{k}|e^{i\bm{q}\hat{\bm{X}}_{k}}|\bm{p}^{\star}_{k}\rangle\delta_{r_{k},r^{\prime}_{k}}.

)

(148)

Then formula (

\displaystyle Q_{k}(\bm{q})=A!\Big[\prod^{A-1}_{l\neq k}(2\pi)^{3}2E_{\bm{p}^{\star}_{l}}\delta^{3}(\bm{p}^{\star}_{l}-\bm{p}^{{}^{\prime}\star}_{l})\delta_{r_{l},r^{\prime}_{l}}\Big]\delta_{r_{k},r^{\prime}_{k}}\sqrt{2E_{\bm{p}^{\star}_{k}}2E_{\bm{p}^{\star}_{k}+\bm{q}}}(2\pi)^{3}\delta^{3}(\bm{p}^{\star}_{k}+\bm{q}-\bm{p}^{{}^{\prime}\star}_{k}),\qquad

where one takes into account that the

.
From formula (

does not depend on the spin indices.

Further, from the factorization of the spin and momentum dependences
(

) and

)
it follows that the value (

The spin structure of the nucleus does not change under the action of the shift operator.
As a result, the product of nuclear wave functions enters into formula

\widetilde{\psi}^{*}_{m}(\{\bm{p}^{\prime}_{\star}\})\chi^{*}_{m}(\{r^{\prime}\})\,\widetilde{\psi}_{n}(\{\bm{p}_{\star}\})\chi_{n}(\{r\})\prod^{A}_{l}\delta_{r_{l},r^{\prime}_{l}}=\widetilde{\psi}^{*}_{m}(\{\bm{p}^{\prime}_{\star}\})\widetilde{\psi}_{n}(\{\bm{p}_{\star}\}).

(150)

Substituting expression (

)

)

),

\displaystyle f^{k}_{mn}(\bm{q})=\int\Bigg[\dfrac{d\bm{p}^{\star}_{k}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\prod^{A}_{i\neq k}\frac{C\,d\bm{p}^{\star}_{i}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\Bigg]\frac{\langle\bm{p}^{{}^{\prime}\star}_{k}|e^{i\bm{q}\hat{\bm{X}}_{k}}|\bm{p}^{\star}_{k}\rangle}{(2\pi)^{3}\sqrt{4E_{\bm{p^{\prime}}^{\star}_{k}}E_{\bm{p}^{\star}_{k}}}}\widetilde{\psi}^{*}_{m}(\{\bm{p}_{\star}\},\bm{p}^{{}^{\prime}\star}_{k}\neq\bm{p}^{\star}_{k})\widetilde{\psi}_{n}(\{\bm{p}_{\star}\})d\bm{p}^{{}^{\prime}\star}_{k}.\qquad

(151)

Taking into account the explicit expression for the one-particle matrix element

\delta(\bm{p}^{\star}_{k}-\bm{p}^{\prime\star}_{k}+\bm{q})
,
and performing subsequent integration over
d\bm{p}^{{}^{\prime}\star}_{k}
,
which leads to cancelation of normalization factors
(2\pi)^{3}\sqrt{4E_{\bm{p^{\prime}}^{\star}_{k}}E_{\bm{p}^{\star}_{k}}}

and to the ”shift” of the argument of the wave function of the final state of the nucleus,
\bm{p}^{\star}_{k}\to\bm{p}^{\star}_{k}+\bm{q}
, one gets

\displaystyle f^{k}_{mn}(\bm{q})=\int\Big[\prod^{A}_{i}\frac{d\bm{p}^{\star}_{i}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\Big]\widetilde{\psi}^{*}_{m}(\{\bm{p}^{(k)}_{\star}\},\bm{p}^{\star}_{k}+\bm{q})\widetilde{\psi}_{n}(\{\bm{p}_{\star}\})(2\pi)^{3}\delta^{3}(\sum^{A}_{i=1}\bm{p}^{\star}_{i}).\quad

(152)

Let us return back to the condition of simultaneous conservation of energy and integrity of the nucleus.
The need to apply this condition to the expression
\langle m|e^{i\bm{q}\hat{\bm{X}}_{k}}|n\rangle
follows from the implicit assumption that the action of the operator
e^{i\bm{q}\hat{\bm{X}}_{k}}
, implemented as a momentum shift of the
k
th nucleon in the initial
|n\rangle
state of the nucleus by
\bm{q}
, does not lead to the disintegration of the nucleus (the nucleus retains its integrity).
Otherwise, the use of the final state of the nucleus in the form of the wave function
\langle m|
becomes meaningless.
Roughly speaking, something from the outside strikes the
k
th nucleon of the nucleus in the
|n\rangle
state,
increasing the momenta of both this nucleon and the entire nucleus by
\bm{q}
, and
as a result, the nucleus, while maintaining its integrity, passes into the
\langle m|
state.
The condition for just such a ”course of things” is relation
(
140
).
Then integration over
\bm{p}^{\star}_{k}
in expression (
152
)
can be removed by the delta function
\delta\big(f(\bm{p^{\star}_{k}})\big)
, and it can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\bar{f}^{k}_{mn}(\bm{q})=\int\Big[\prod^{A}_{i}\frac{d\bm{p}^{\star}_{i}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\delta\big(f(\bm{p^{\star}_{k}})\big)\Big]\widetilde{\psi}^{*}_{m}(\{\bm{p}^{(k)}_{\star}\},\bm{p^{\star}}_{k}+\bm{q})\widetilde{\psi}_{n}(\{\bm{p}^{\star}\})(2\pi)^{3}\delta^{3}(\sum^{A}_{i=1}\bm{p}^{\star}_{i}).\quad

(153)

Note that expression (
153
) for
\bm{q}=0

turns into the condition for the normalization of nuclear states in the rest frame of the nucleus
(
124
).
Indeed, at
\bm{q}=0
one has
q_{0}=0
, and from the condition
\delta(q_{0}+P_{0,n}-P^{\prime}_{0,m})=1

follows
P_{0,n}-P^{\prime}_{0,m}=-T_{A}-\Delta\varepsilon_{mn}=0
, i.e.,

f(\bm{p^{\star}_{k}})=-T_{A}-\Delta\varepsilon_{mn}+\sqrt{m^{2}+{\bm{p^{\star}_{k}}}^{2}}-\sqrt{m^{2}+{\bm{p^{\star}_{k}}}^{2}}\equiv-T_{A}-\Delta\varepsilon_{mn}=0\quad\text{for any}\ \ \bm{p^{\star}_{k}}.

So
\delta(f(\bm{p^{\star}_{k}}))\equiv 1
can be taken out of the integral over the variable

\bm{p^{\star}_{k}}
, and formula (
153
) becomes

\displaystyle\bar{f}^{k}_{mn}(\bm{0})=\langle m|e^{i\bm{0}\hat{\bm{X}}_{k}}|n\rangle=\int\Big[\prod^{A}_{i}\frac{d\bm{p}^{\star}_{i}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\Big]\widetilde{\psi}^{*}_{m}(\{\bm{p}^{(k)}_{\star}\},\bm{p^{\star}}_{k})\widetilde{\psi}_{n}(\{\bm{p}^{\star}\})(2\pi)^{3}\delta^{3}(\sum^{A}_{i=1}\bm{p}^{\star}_{i})\equiv\langle m|n\rangle=\delta_{mn}.

Therefore, formula (
153
) has the same meaning as expression (
152
), being without the delta function.

Using formula (
153
) for
\bar{f}^{k}_{mn}(\bm{q})
,
one can write the hadronic current (
141
) as

\displaystyle\bar{h}^{\mu}_{mn}(\bm{q})=\sum^{A}_{k}\frac{\overline{u}(\bm{\bar{p}^{\star}_{k}}+\bm{q},r^{\prime}_{k})O^{\mu}_{k}\,u(\bm{\bar{p}^{\star}_{k}},r_{k})}{\sqrt{4E_{\bm{\bar{p}^{\star}_{k}}}E_{\bm{\bar{p}^{\star}_{k}}+\bm{q}}}}\bar{f}^{k}_{mn}(\bm{q})\,\lambda^{mn}(r^{\prime},r),

(154)

where
\bm{\bar{p}^{\star}_{k}}
is the solution of equation (
140
).
It is worth comparing expression (
154
)
with a similar formula from
Bednyakov:2018mjd
;
Bednyakov:2021ppn
that
has the form

h^{\mu}_{mn}(\bm{q})=\sum_{k=1}^{A}\frac{\bar{u}(\bar{\bm{p}}+\bm{q},r^{\prime}_{k})O^{\mu}_{k}u(\bar{\bm{p}},r_{k})}{\sqrt{4E_{\bar{\bm{p}}}E_{\bar{\bm{p}}+\bm{q}}}}{f}^{k}_{mn}(\bm{q})\lambda^{mn}(r^{\prime},r),\quad\text{where}

{f}^{k}_{mn}(\bm{q})\equiv\int\Big[\prod_{j=1}^{A}\frac{d\bm{p}^{\star}_{j}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\Big]\widetilde{\psi}_{m}^{*}(\{\bm{p}^{(k)}_{\star}\})\widetilde{\psi}_{n}(\{\bm{p}_{\star}\})(2\pi)^{3}\delta^{3}(\sum_{l=1}^{A}\bm{p}^{\star}_{l}).

(155)

To obtain
this
expression, a
special assumption
was made in

Bednyakov:2018mjd
;
Bednyakov:2021ppn
about the possibility of taking
the single-particle matrix element,

\dfrac{\bar{u}(\bar{\bm{p}}+\bm{q},r^{\prime}_{k})O^{\mu}_{k}u(\bar{\bm{p}},r_{k})}{\sqrt{4E_{\bar{\bm{p}}}E_{\bar{\bm{p}}+\bm{q}}}}
, out of the integration right at the momentum
\bar{\bm{p}}
,
being the solution of equation (
140
).
In formula (
154
)
this factor is obtained automatically, but one has to slightly redefine the form factor function,
i.e., instead of

from (

one takes
\bar{f}^{k}_{mn}(\bm{q})
from (

).
Nevertheless, this ”redefinition” does not play any role,
since the ”physical”nuclear form factors of protons and neutrons

F_{p/n}(\bm{q})
are defined in terms of the functions
{f}^{k}_{mn}(\bm{q})
only formally
(see, for example formula (

i.e.,
without explicit calculation
of the above-mentioned multidimensional integrals.

Based on the definition of
f^{k}_{mn}(\bm{q})
, for example in the
form of (

),
and on the symmetry properties of the nuclear wave function, one can conclude

that the structure factor

does not depend on the number

,
and depends only on whether this

corresponds to the proton or the neutron.
This property is used below.

Coherent and incoherent terms of the cross section
{\chi A\to\chi A^{(*)}}
.

The measurable differential cross section can be obtained by averaging (over all possible initial nucleus states

|n\rangle
) and summing (over all possible final states
|m\rangle
)
of the differential cross section, which corresponds to the nuclear transition from the initial state
|n\rangle

to the final state
|m\rangle
due to the interaction with the
\chi
particle (

)

\frac{d\sigma}{dT_{A}}(\chi A\to\chi A^{(*)})=\sum_{n,m}\omega_{n}\dfrac{|{i\cal M}_{mn}|^{2}}{2^{5}\pi|\bm{k^{l}_{\chi}}|^{2}m_{A}}C_{mn},\quad\text{where}\quad\sum_{n}\omega_{n}=1.

(156)

With the matrix element from (
11
)
summed over the nucleon spin indices
Bednyakov:2018mjd
;

;

\displaystyle i\mathcal{M}_{mn}^{s^{\prime}s}=i\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}\frac{m_{A}}{m_{n}}C_{1,mn}^{1/2}\sum_{k=1}^{A}\sum_{r^{\prime}r}f^{k}_{mn}\lambda^{mn}(r^{\prime},r)(l_{s^{\prime}s}\,h^{k}_{r^{\prime}r}),

(157)

the differential cross section (

)
can be written as a sum of two terms from

)

\displaystyle T^{s^{\prime}s}_{m=n}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle g_{\text{c}}\sum^{A}_{k,j}\sum_{n}\omega_{n}\Big[f^{k}_{nn}f^{j*}_{nn}\sum_{r}(l_{s^{\prime}s}\,h^{k}_{rr})\sum_{x}(l_{s^{\prime}s}\,h^{j}_{xx})^{*}\Big]\text{~~and~~}

(158)

\displaystyle T^{s^{\prime}s}_{m\neq n}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle g_{\text{i}}\sum^{A}_{k,j}\sum_{n}\omega_{n}\Big[\sum_{m\neq n}f^{k}_{mn}f^{j*}_{mn}\sum_{r^{\prime}r}\lambda^{mn}_{r^{\prime}r}(l_{s^{\prime}s}\,h^{k}_{r^{\prime}r})\Big(\sum_{x^{\prime}x}\lambda^{mn}_{x^{\prime}x}(l_{s^{\prime}s}\,h^{j}_{x^{\prime}x})\Big)^{{\dagger}}\Big].\qquad

(159)

Here, correction factors are introduced in the form

\displaystyle g_{\text{i/c}}\equiv C_{1,mn}C_{mn}\simeq\dfrac{\Big(1+\dfrac{\varepsilon_{n}}{m_{A}}\Big)\Big(1+\dfrac{\varepsilon_{m}+T_{A}}{m_{A}}\Big)}{\sqrt{1+\dfrac{\bar{\bm{p}}^{2}}{m^{2}}}\Big(\sqrt{1+\dfrac{\bar{\bm{p}}^{2}}{m^{2}}}+\dfrac{T_{A}+\Delta\varepsilon_{mn}}{m}\Big)}\dfrac{\Big(1+\dfrac{T_{A}}{m_{A}}\Big)/\Big(1+\dfrac{T_{A}+\varepsilon_{m}}{m_{A}}\Big)}{1+\dfrac{\varepsilon_{n}}{m_{A}}\Big(1+\dfrac{m^{2}_{\chi}}{|\bm{k^{l}_{\chi}}|^{2}}\Big)}.\qquad

(160)

The factors differ from 1 at a level of
O(10^{-3})
and with the same accuracy
they do not depend on the nuclear indices
m,n
and the recoil energy
T_{A}
14
14

14

Since in the accepted approximation
one has
\ \dfrac{\varepsilon_{m}+T_{A}}{m_{A}}\leq 10^{-3}
,

\dfrac{\Delta\varepsilon_{mn}+T_{A}}{m}\leq 10^{-3}
and
\dfrac{|\bar{\bm{p}}|^{2}}{m^{2}}\leq 0.01
.

. For this reason, the summation over the index
n
in formula
(
158
)
can be interpreted as the appearance of the form factors averaged over all possible initial states of the nucleus,
which are given by formula (
30
)
from the main text.
This allows expression (
158
)
to be separated into contributions of protons and neutrons as follows:

\displaystyle\dfrac{T^{s^{\prime}s}_{m=n}}{g_{\text{c}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum^{Z}_{k,j}F_{p}\sum^{2}_{r=1}(l_{s^{\prime}s}\,h^{p}_{rr}){F^{*}_{p}}\sum^{2}_{x=1}(l_{s^{\prime}s}\,h^{p}_{xx})^{*}+\sum^{N}_{k,j}F_{n}\sum^{2}_{r=1}(l_{s^{\prime}s}\,h^{n}_{rr}){F^{*}_{n}}\sum^{2}_{x=1}(l_{s^{\prime}s}\,h^{n}_{xx})^{*}+

\displaystyle+\sum^{Z}_{k}\sum^{N}_{j}F_{p}\sum^{2}_{r=1}(l_{s^{\prime}s}\,h^{p}_{rr})F_{n}^{*}\sum^{2}_{x=1}(l_{s^{\prime}s}\,h^{n}_{xx})^{*}+\sum^{N}_{k}\sum^{Z}_{j}F_{n}\sum^{2}_{r=1}(l_{s^{\prime}s}\,h^{n}_{rr})F_{p}^{*}\sum^{2}_{x=1}(l_{s^{\prime}s}\,h^{p}_{xx})^{*}.

The formula can be presented as the squared modulus of the sum over proton and neutron contributions:

\displaystyle T^{s^{\prime}s}_{m=n}=g_{\text{c}}\Big|\sum^{Z}_{k}\sum_{r}(l_{s^{\prime}s}\,h^{p}_{rr})F_{p}+\sum^{N}_{j}\sum_{r}(l_{s^{\prime}s}\,h^{n}_{rr})F_{n}\Big|^{2}=g_{\text{c}}\Big|\sum_{f=p,n}\sum^{A_{f}}_{k=1}\sum_{r}(l_{s^{\prime}s}\,h^{f}_{rr})F_{f}\Big|^{2}.\qquad

(161)

The second term from (
27
), or
expression (
159
),
contains summation over both indices
m,n
.
To carry out this summation and to continue transformations of the term
T_{m\neq n}
,
certain assumptions should be made about the unknown behavior of
\lambda^{mn}_{r^{\prime}r}
.
Recall that the spin functions of the nucleus are normalized by condition
(
20
)

\chi_{m}^{*}(\{r\})\chi_{n}(\{r\})=\delta_{nm},\quad\chi_{n}^{*}(\{r^{\prime}\})\chi_{n}(\{r\})=\delta_{\{r^{\prime}\}\{r^{\prime}\}}.

The definition (
21
) was adopted
for the products of the spin functions.
Since formula (
21
) is valid for any value of
k
,
the index
k
of
r^{\prime}_{k}
and
r_{k}
was omitted.
If one keeps the index
k
, expression (
21
) takes the form

\chi^{*}_{m}(\{r^{(k)}\},r^{\prime}_{k})\chi_{n}(\{r\},r_{k})\equiv\lambda^{mn}(r^{\prime}_{k},r_{k})=\delta_{mn}\delta_{r^{\prime}_{k}r_{k}}+(1-\delta_{mn})\lambda^{mn}_{r^{\prime}_{k}r_{k}}.

Here

\{r\}\equiv\{r_{1},r_{2},..,r_{k},..,r_{A}\}
is the set of all nucleon spin projections in the initial nucleus
A
,
and
\{r^{(k)}\}
is the similar set for the final nucleus,
differing only by that the
k
th place in the set
\{r^{(k)}\}
is occupied by another number
r^{\prime}_{k}

not equal to
r_{k}
from
\{r\}
.
All other projection values are the same.
In other words,
\{r^{(k)}\}\equiv\{r_{1},r_{2},..,r^{\prime}_{k},..,r_{A}\}
.
It can be seen that for
m=n
one has

\chi^{*}_{n}(\{r^{(k)}\},r^{\prime}_{k})\chi_{n}(\{r\},r_{k})=\delta_{r^{\prime}_{k}r_{k}},

(162)

i.e., all values in two sets of spin index variables
\{r^{(k)}\}
and
\{r\}
must fully coincide
if the spin functions refer to the same (
n
th) internal quantum state of the nucleus.
In other words, if the nucleus has not changed, then all values of the spin indices of the nucleus

\{r\}
should also remain unchanged.

Next, consider the product of spin functions
included in the squared matrix element
(
159
)

for the same
active
k
th nucleon when
m\neq n
.
It is

\lambda^{mn}_{r^{\prime}_{k}r_{k}}[\lambda^{mn}_{x^{\prime}_{k}x_{k}}]^{*}=\chi^{*}_{m}(\{r^{(k)}\},r^{\prime}_{k})\chi_{n}(\{r\},r_{k})\cdot[\chi^{*}_{m}(\{x^{(k)}\},x^{\prime}_{k})\chi_{n}(\{x\},x_{k})]^{*}.

Performing complex conjugation and rearranging the functions, one gets

\lambda^{mn}_{r^{\prime}_{k},r_{k}}[\lambda^{mn}_{x^{\prime}_{k},x_{k}}]^{*}=\big[\chi^{*}_{m}(\{r^{(k)}\},r^{\prime}_{k})\chi_{m}(\{x^{(k)}\},x^{\prime}_{k})\big]\big[\chi_{n}(\{r\},r_{k})\chi^{*}_{n}(\{x\},x_{k})\big].

Then, according to (
162
),
for each of these two products corresponding to the same
state of the nucleus (
m
and
n
, respectively) to be different from zero,
the sets of spin indices in each pair of products must completely coincide, i.e.,

\{r^{(k)}\}=\{x^{(k)}\}
and
\{r\}=\{x\}
.
In other words,
for any

k
there should be the following:

\chi^{*}_{m}(\{r^{(k)}\},r^{\prime}_{k})\chi_{m}(\{x^{(k)}\},x^{\prime}_{k})=\delta_{r^{\prime}_{k},x^{\prime}_{k}}\quad\text{and}\quad\chi_{n}(\{r\},r_{k})\chi^{*}_{n}(\{x\},x_{k})=\delta_{r_{k},x_{k}}.

As a result, it turns out that

[\lambda^{mn}_{r^{\prime}_{k}r_{k}}][\lambda^{mn}_{x^{\prime}_{k}x_{k}}]^{*}=\delta_{r^{\prime}_{k},x^{\prime}_{k}}\delta_{r_{k},x_{k}},\quad\text{and}\quad|\lambda^{mn}_{r^{\prime}_{k}r_{k}}|^{2}=1.

(163)

Here the index
k
explicitly takes into account the invariance of the type and number of the active nucleon.
The last equality in (
163
)
means that in the case of scattering
on the same active nucleon
, regardless of its number
k
and
of the change in the state of the nucleus
|n\rangle\to|m\rangle
,
any final orientation of the spin of this nucleon (index
r^{\prime}_{k}
) is equally possible
for any initial orientation of the spin of the active nucleon (index
r_{k}
).
Formally, this result is a consequence of the
normalization conditions for the spin wave functions
(
162
).
There is also no operator that changes the spin of the nucleon and
remains standing
between these spin functions, preventing their normalization condition ”to work”.
Indeed, if the
interaction did not occur
, then the initial
|n\rangle
and final
|m\rangle
states are completely independent.
In these states there should be no correlation between the spin directions
of the ”active” nucleon (the concept of which is undefined in such a situation).
When
the interaction
occured, transferring the nucleus from the
|n\rangle
state to
the
|m\rangle
state, a correlation arises between the
r
- and
r^{\prime}
-spins of the active nucleon,

the intensity
of which (up to zero) is regulated by the scalar product
(l_{s^{\prime}s}\,h_{r^{\prime}r})
.

Thus,
it can be accepted
that the products of
\lambda^{mn}_{r^{\prime}r}

corresponding to the scattering on the same active nucleon
do not depend on the indices
m
and
n
and are equal to some averaged values for protons and neutrons
15
15

15

When
x_{k}=r_{k}
and
x^{\prime}_{k}=r^{\prime}_{k}
, the physical meaning of the square
|\lambda^{mn}_{r^{\prime}_{k}r_{k}}|^{2}\equiv\lambda^{mn}_{r^{\prime}_{k}r_{k}}[\lambda^{mn}_{x^{\prime}_{k}=r^{\prime}_{k},x_{k}=r_{k}}]^{*}
is clear. It is the probability for the
k
th nucleon to meet the lepton with its spin projection
r_{k}
and sent the lepton away with the spin projection
r^{\prime}_{k}
, provided that the nucleus passes from the
|n\rangle
state to the
|m\rangle
state. The meaning of the product
\lambda^{mn}_{+-}[\lambda^{mn}_{++}]^{*}
, where the initial spin index
r_{k}=-1
does not coincide with the initial spin index
x_{k}=+1
, is unclear,
if one considers the same active nucleon
. Such a situation can apparently arise when
\lambda^{mn}_{+-}
refers to one nucleon, and
[\lambda^{mn}_{++}]^{*}
refers
to another
one, i.e., when
two different active nucleons
participate in the scattering process.
As shown below, such a scenario contributes negligibly.

\lambda^{mn}_{r^{\prime}r}\simeq\lambda^{p/n}_{r^{\prime}r}.

(164)

Therefore, due to (

), the

.

In deriving formulas (

Using the completeness condition

\displaystyle\sum_{m\neq n}f_{mn}^{k}f_{mn}^{j*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{m}\langle n|e^{i\bm{q}\bm{X}_{k}}|m\rangle\langle m|e^{-i\bm{q}\bm{X}_{j}}|n\rangle-\sum_{m=n}\langle n|e^{i\bm{q}\bm{X}_{k}}|m\rangle\langle m|e^{-i\bm{q}\bm{X}_{j}}|n\rangle=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle n|e^{i\bm{q}\hat{\bm{X}}_{k}}e^{-i\bm{q}\hat{\bm{X}}_{j}}|n\rangle-\langle n|e^{i\bm{q}\hat{\bm{X}}_{k}}|n\rangle\langle n|e^{-i\bm{q}\hat{\bm{X}}_{j}}|n\rangle.

Expression (
169
) vanishes both for small transferred momenta
\bm{q}\to 0
and for large
\bm{q}\to\infty
, i.e.,

\lim_{\bm{q}\to 0}\langle\text{cov}(e^{-i\bm{q}\hat{\bm{X}}_{j}},e^{i\bm{q}\hat{\bm{X}}_{k}})\rangle_{p}=0
and

\lim_{\bm{q}\to\infty}\langle\text{cov}(e^{-i\bm{q}\hat{\bm{X}}_{j}},e^{i\bm{q}\hat{\bm{X}}_{k}})\rangle_{p}=0.

A similar consideration is carried out for
different
neutrons (the 3rd line in
formulas (
165
)) and
is generalized to the joint case of protons and neutrons (the last line in formula
(
165
)).
Finally, the ”incoherent” term (
159
)
appears as expression (
36
) in the main text.
